### 4.16 BG-FLAGS rows (Step 7: background input with Chrome restarted under flags; decisions.md #64 (2))

*Added 2026-10-23 by stage BG-FLAGS, before Chrome was touched and before any BG-FLAGS measurement
(decisions.md · 2026-10-23 · The driver may reload the extension by UI automation, and restart Chrome
with flags for the background-input test (#64) (2); · 2026-10-22 · BG-1/BG-2's focus-emulation retry
cannot run in this wave; T5.2 reports it unmet if they fail (#61); · 2026-10-23 · The user rules on
Run 8: fix stage T5b, then Run 9 (#62) item (4)). Run 8's BG results (§5 Run 8) stay
as recorded. The order and set-up are in the wave sheet
[`T5-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-21-step7-multi-actor-tabs-steps/T5-wave-sheet.md) § "BG-FLAGS
(Run 10)". §4.14's tools, markers and cautions B1–B6 apply; tags as in §4.12.*

**The question.** Run 8: a trusted click / trusted typing into a background tab answers `ok` in 5–10 ms
and the page receives nothing; after `focusTab` both work (BG-1/BG-2 FAIL). Is Chrome's background
throttling the cause? This stage measures it **with no code change**: Chrome is restarted with flags
that disable renderer backgrounding, and BG-1/BG-2 are run again exactly as written. Focus emulation
proper (`Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled`) needs a bridge method that does not exist; adding it is
Step 8's (#62 (4)) and is **not** done here — BGF-1/BGF-2 record only whether these flags alone change
the outcome.

**The flag set** (as the stage brief gives it; unchanged):
`--disable-renderer-backgrounding --disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows --disable-background-timer-throttling`.
What each does: the first keeps a background tab's renderer process at normal priority; the second
stops Chrome treating a covered window's tabs as backgrounded; the third stops the 1 s timer clamp of
background pages. None of them makes a background tab *visible* or *focused* — so `document.visibilityState`
of a background tab is still expected to read `hidden`, which BG-1/BG-2's PASS rule requires anyway.

**New helpers** (written before the run; Windows PowerShell 5.1; counts only — none prints a title or URL):
- `recipes/bgf-count.ps1 [-CloseBlank -Expect <N>]` — one line: `chrome.exe` processes, browser processes,
  browser windows (visible `Chrome_WidgetWin_1` windows of `chrome.exe` titled `… - Google Chrome`),
  how many minimized, tabs (UIA elements of ControlType `TabItem` **and** ClassName `Tab` per window —
  the tab strip only; a page's `role="tab"` elements are also `TabItem`s, with an empty ClassName, and
  are not counted (prereg verifier finding 1, measured: the user's window read 2 `TabItem`s = 1 `Tab` +
  1 web element); total and the per-window list sorted), `blank windows` (windows titled exactly
  `about:blank - Google Chrome` with one tab) and the browser process's switches (names only).
  `-CloseBlank -Expect <N>` closes the blank window (WM_CLOSE) only when exactly one exists **and** there
  are exactly N + 1 browser windows, then counts again; otherwise it closes nothing.
- `recipes/bgf-quit.ps1` — quits Chrome **gracefully**: opens one window of its own on `about:blank`
  (as `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` does), checks its address bar reads `about:blank`, sets it to
  `chrome://quit` and sends one Enter only while that window is in front with its address bar focused
  (Chrome's own Exit: every window closes and the session is saved), then waits ≤ 60 s for no
  `chrome.exe` to remain. **It never kills `chrome.exe`**: exit 3 if Chrome is still running, and the
  driver stops. Exit 2 = nothing sent.
- `recipes/bgf-launch.ps1 -Exe <chrome.exe> -ArgLine '<args>'` — starts Chrome outside the driver's job
  object (the driver's shell runs in one; limit flags `0x800`, measured) with the user's default
  environment (`CreateEnvironmentBlock`; the driver's session variables are not passed on) and no
  inherited handles, in a new process group (`CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP`, so no console Ctrl+C of the
  driver reaches it), as a desktop shortcut would. Exit 2 = refused, nothing started.

**The restart and restore procedure (both restarts).** Pre-registered, because it is the user's real
Chrome (integrity floor):
0. **Precondition (restart 1 only):** the pre-flight check's `listTabs — <k> tab(s)` (one check
   window open) equals T + 1 — the two tab counts agree. Otherwise `bgf-count.ps1` may get a set-up fix
   (recorded with its diff) and the first count is taken again; if they still disagree ⇒ STOP before
   any restart.
1. All the driver's own windows are closed; `bgf-count` must print the same `browser windows`, `tabs`
   and per-window list as the stage's first count (N windows, T tabs, list P), with `blank windows 0`
   — else ⇒ STOP (no quit). Chrome's path and version are recorded
   (`C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe`, file version `156.0.8078.48` measured
   at 23:12Z).
2. `bgf-quit.ps1` → `QUIT OK - no chrome.exe process remains, <s> s after the Enter`, exit 0. Exit 3
   ⇒ **STOP** (nothing is forced; the record says so). Exit 2 ⇒ nothing was sent: step 1's count is
   taken again (a window the script left open would change it ⇒ STOP), then one rerun; a second exit 2
   ⇒ STOP.
3. `bgf-launch.ps1 -Exe 'C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe' -ArgLine '<args>'` with
   `<args>` = `--restore-last-session` followed by the flag set (restart 1) or `--restore-last-session`
   alone (restart 2) → `bgf-launch: started pid <p> …; in job: False; …`.
4. `bgf-count` every 2 s until two results in a row are equal and show ≥ N browser windows, at most 60 s
   (at 60 s the last count is judged as it stands).
   The quit window is in the saved session, so it may come back: **expected** N + 1 windows with
   `blank windows 1`, or N windows with `blank windows 0`. Then `bgf-count -CloseBlank -Expect N` (only
   if blank = 1 and windows = N + 1) closes that one window. **Restore PASS** = the final count prints exactly N browser windows,
   T tabs, per-window list P, `blank windows 0`, and `browser switches: [<args>]` (the flags present
   after restart 1; only `--restore-last-session` after restart 2). Anything else — fewer or more
   windows or tabs, a different list, two blank windows — ⇒ **STOP all further steps** and report it
   plainly; the driver does not try to repair the user's session (nothing is reopened or closed). The
   record then names what the STOP leaves behind: whether Chrome runs, with which switches, and whether
   the driver's blank window is still open.
5. `npx tsx recipes/t5.ts check` in a driver window on `…?t5=check` → `CHECK PASS — …` (the extension
   reconnects to the daemon by itself after a restart; its `listTabs — <k> tab(s)` must equal the
   pre-flight check's `<k>`, taken with the same one check window open — a second, independent tab
   count). Only if it fails with the extension not connected after 30 s: `reload-extension.ps1`
   (#65), then the check once more; a second failure ⇒ STOP (restart 1: then restart 2 runs at once to
   take the flags off; restart 2: report).
At the end Chrome must be as it was: N windows, T tabs, list P, none of the three flags (the first
count's switches were `[]`), `t5.ts check` passing. Two harmless differences are expected and recorded:
the command line keeps `--restore-last-session` (a one-shot switch), and the browser process is no
longer in a job object (the original one was, with its parent gone).

**Validity of BGF-0…5.** BGF-1…4 count only if `bgf-count`, run right before them, prints the three
flags in `browser switches`; BGF-0 and BGF-5 only if it prints none of them. Otherwise the row is
**not run (set-up)**, neither PASS nor FAIL.

| ID | Objective | Steps | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| BGF-0 | Baseline before restart 1: BG-1/BG-2 still fail on this build without flags (informational; makes the comparison discriminating) | Driver window Wb on `…?t5=bg-human`, topmost, current (B1, B4). **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/t5.ts bg1 --then-focus`; `cleanup --keep bg-human`; B1; `bg2 --then-focus`; `cleanup --keep bg-human`. | Recorded either way, judged by BG-1's / BG-2's rules (§4.14). Run 8 recorded both `FAIL` (`ok` in 5–10 ms, `status: idle`). |
| BGF-1 ★ | BG-1 under the flag set: trusted click into a background tab | Restart 1 done (Restore PASS) and the check passed. Driver window Wb on `…?t5=bg-human`, `Set-T5Topmost`, current (B1, B4); `bgf-count` shows the flags. **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/t5.ts bg1 --then-focus`. | BG-1's rule (§4.14), copied: PASS = `click e<N> trusted → ok (<ms> ms)`; `target status "status: TRUSTED click received"; …; target visibilityState hidden`; `human tab hidden during the call: no; after — human … active in its window, current; target … background …`; `BG-1 PASS — exact; background throughout: yes; human's tab unchanged: yes` (the script's PASS also requires the target `hidden` when read). On FAIL the text is recorded, and so is the script's line `BG-1 focus-emulation retry: not run — no bridge method sends Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled, and adding one is an extension change (forbidden in a wave)` (focus emulation is Step 8's, #62 (4)). The `--then-focus` line (`informational, not the T5.2 retry — after focusTab …`) is recorded either way. |
| BGF-2 ★ | BG-2 under the flag set: trusted typing into a background tab | As BGF-1, after `cleanup --keep bg-human` and B1 again: `npx tsx recipes/t5.ts bg2 --then-focus`. | BG-2's rule (§4.14), copied: PASS = `type e<N> "bg2typed" trusted → ok (<ms> ms)`; `target status "status: TRUSTED input = bg2typed"; field value "bg2typed"; target visibilityState hidden`; the human's-tab line as BG-1; `BG-2 PASS — exact; …`. FAIL handling as BGF-1. |
| BGF-3 | Control under the flags: clipboard write needs focus (BG-3) | As BG-3 (§4.14) in Wb: **[SH]** `t5.ts open bg3`; `t5.ts info bg3` → `background`. **[UI]** actor F (`--tools 'browser_select_tab,browser_evaluate,browser_focus_tab'`): `browser_select_tab {"tab":"<T#>"}`; `browser_evaluate {"expression":"navigator.clipboard.writeText('t5-bg3').then(() => 'ok')"}`; `browser_focus_tab {}`; the same `browser_evaluate` again. | BG-3's rule, copied: Select: `Selected tab <T#> (…)`. First evaluate: an error (`OUTPUT (isError)`) containing `NotAllowedError` or `Document is not focused`. Focus: `Focused tab <T#>`. Second evaluate: `ok`. (The user's clipboard then holds `t5-bg3`.) |
| BGF-4 | Control under the flags: `waitFor {text}` and `networkIdle` on a background tab (BG-4) | As BGF-1's set-up. **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/t5.ts bg4`. | BG-4's rule, copied: `… target <T#> (background, visibilityState hidden); the text is added 2000 ms after the arm`; `waitFor {text} → ok after <t> ms` with 1900 ≤ `<t>` ≤ 10000; `fetch /ok.json → 200; waitFor {networkIdle} → ok after <n> ms` with `<n>` ≤ 10000; `BG-4 PASS — text after <t> ms, network idle after <n> ms; target still background: yes`. The elapsed times are recorded (Run 8: 3746 ms / 552 ms; with the timer clamp off, `<t>` nearer 2000 ms is expected but not required). |
| BGF-5 | After restart 2 (no flags): BG-1/BG-2 once more (informational) | Restart 2 done (Restore PASS, `browser switches: [--restore-last-session]`), the check passed; Wb as BGF-1. **[SH]** `bg1 --then-focus`; `cleanup --keep bg-human`; B1; `bg2 --then-focus`. | Recorded either way, judged by BG-1's / BG-2's rules. |

**Reading the result (pre-registered).** The flags **change** BG-1 (resp. BG-2) iff BGF-0's row FAILs,
BGF-1 (BGF-2) PASSes, and BGF-5's row FAILs again. BGF-1/BGF-2 FAIL ⇒ "the flags alone do not change
the outcome" (background throttling is not the cause on its own; focus emulation stays Step 8's
question). BGF-1/BGF-2 PASS but BGF-5 also PASSes ⇒ "not attributable to the flags" (the restart or
something else changed it). BGF-0 PASS ⇒ the baseline no longer reproduces Run 8: recorded, and the
flags' effect is "not determinable"; the same reading if BGF-0, BGF-1/BGF-2 or BGF-5 is not run. One
trial per condition (as in Run 8); a single PASS/FAIL is reported as such. Every outcome is fine;
nothing is re-tuned after the measurement.

### 4.17 FE rows (Step 8: focus emulation for trusted input; decisions.md #71, #78, #85 item 3)

*Added 2026-10-23 by Step 8 stage FE's builder, before any FE build was loaded and before any FE row
ran (exit check FE.4; decisions.md · 2026-10-23 · Stage FE measures focus emulation before `policy.ts`
is set (#71), amended by · FE's rows and outcome table, amended before anything is measured (#78) and
· Stage 0's build-level rulings: SDK-1's recipe, S3.5's control, the stage split (#85) item 3). The
order, the set-up and the commands are in the wave sheet
[`FE-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-21-step8-sdk-surface-and-mcp-cursor-steps/FE-wave-sheet.md).
§4.14's markers and cautions B1–B6 apply; tags as in §4.12. Run 8's and Run 10's BG results stay as
recorded.*

**The question.** Trusted CDP input into a background tab answers `ok` and the page receives nothing
(BG-1/BG-2, Run 8; BGF-1/BGF-2, Run 10). Stage FE adds `focusEmulation: true` to `click`/`type`/`pressKey`
on the trusted path (explicit `trusted:true`, or click/type's escalation after a content-action
failure): in the same debugger session the extension sends `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled
{enabled:true}`, then (for `type`) focuses the field, then the unchanged `Input.*` commands, then
`{enabled:false}` in a `finally`, then detaches; it focuses no window and activates no tab, and the
result carries `focusEmulation: "on"`. Without the param every call is unchanged. These rows measure
whether the page then receives the input, and what the human sees. By Chromium's source the target
page reads `visible` while emulation is on (stage0-audit.md F1, §8; unconfirmed for Chrome 156): that is
recorded as an observation, not judged.

**Tools of this section** (in `recipes/`, written before the run; `npx tsc --noEmit -p
recipes/tsconfig.fe.json` exit 0; `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts selftest` → `SELFTEST PASS — 79/79 checks as
expected`, the dry run of every rule on canned good and bad inputs, connecting to nothing):
- `fe.ts <command>` — one command per row (its header lists them). Every line is `<ts> <name>:
  <message>` (the t5 format); each row prints its own `… PASS` / `… FAIL` line, computed by the pure
  functions of `fe-rules.ts`, and appends one JSON record per FE-0, positive control, capture judgement
  and trial to `$FE_OUT\fe-results.jsonl` (handles, window ids, the fixture's status line, field value
  and visibility logs — never a URL or a title). `fe.ts verdict` re-computes every rule from that file
  and prints the outcome by the table below. It prints only fixture URLs (none, in practice).
- `fe-win.ps1 -Hwnd <Wf> -Seconds <s> -IntervalMs 50 -Csv <file> [-StopFile <file>]` — the window-capture
  loop: one `PrintWindow` capture of the driver's window every ~50 ms (~140 ms per frame was measured
  on a scratch window at `-IntervalMs 100`), writing per frame the time, the **foreground window's
  handle**, the size, and the colours of the uniform rows at the top of the window (three probes per
  row, right of the bars' labels); no image is written and no title printed. `fe.ts frames <csv> --for
  positive|block` judges it.
- Each trial runs on a **fresh background fixture tab** `…?t5=<row>-<kind>-<k>` opened by the script
  (`newTab`, `active:false`, leased to the script) in the driver's window and closed after the trial,
  so no trial can read an earlier trial's status line (#78). The human's tab is the driver's
  `…?t5=fe-human` tab (cyan bar `HUMAN`); each target gets a magenta bar `<row> TARGET`. Both carry the
  `visibilitychange` logger (`VISLOG_INSTALL`, page-side epoch timestamps as SCREEN-1). During each call a
  `listTabs` poller (lock-free) reads every 25 ms, from 50 ms before the call to 50 ms after it.

**Order** (the wave sheet's): reload (exit 0) → FE-0 → FE-5 first → the positive control → FE-1, FE-2,
FE-3 under one capture loop → FE-5 last → FE-3c (observation) → `verdict`.

| ID | Objective | Steps | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| FE-0 ★ | The loaded extension is the FE build (the run's first row after `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` exit 0) | Driver window Wf on `…?t5=fe-human`, current (B1), uncovered (B4). **[SH]** `Get-DaemonBuild`; `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts check`. | `fe-check: connected as client c<k> — daemon build <sha>, proto 2, extension connected` (`<sha>` = `Get-DaemonBuild`, recorded); `fe-check: human <T#h> (fixture page); target <T#x> (background, leased to fe-check)`; `fe-check: click e<N> trusted focusEmulation:true → ok {"ok":true,"focusEmulation":"on"} (<ms> ms)`; `fe-check: FE-0 PASS — the result carries focusEmulation "on": the extension is the FE build`. PASS = the result carries `focusEmulation: "on"`. A stale extension ignores the param and answers `ok {"ok":true}` → `FE-0 FAIL — no focusEmulation "on" in the result ⇒ stop, reload, re-check (not an outcome; #78)`: **FAIL is not an outcome** — stop, reload, re-check; no other row runs before an FE-0 PASS. Whether the target received the click is not judged here. |
| FE-5 ★ | Control: BG-1 and BG-2 exactly as §4.14, **without** the option, on the same build; run **first** (before FE-1) and **last** (after FE-3) | As FE-0's set-up. **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts control --phase first`; later `… control --phase last`. Three BG-1 trials (trusted click on `Trusted-only button`) then three BG-2 trials (trusted `type "bg2typed"` into `Trusted-only input`), each on a fresh tab. | Per trial: `fe-5: FE-5 (<phase>) BG-<1\|2> trial <k>: …` lines as FE-1's (with no `focusEmulation:true` and a result `ok {"ok":true}`), then `fe-5: FE-5 (<phase>) BG-<1\|2> trial <k>: BG-<1\|2>'s rule <PASS\|FAIL>; received input: <yes\|no>`; finally `fe-5: FE-5 control (<phase>) — BG-1 received input in <a>/3 trials, BG-2 in <b>/3; control <holds\|VOID (received input)>`. "Received input" = the target's status line is not `status: idle` **or** its field is not empty, read after the call (any page-side effect counts). **FE-5 "passes" — voids the comparison — if the control receives input in ANY trial of either block** (#78); expected (Runs 8, 10): `holds`, `0/3` and `0/3` twice. |
| FE-PC ★ | Positive control (#78 item 3): the human's-tab logger, and the capture loop, see a real switch | As FE-0's set-up. **[SH]** start `fe-win.ps1 -Hwnd <Wf> -Seconds 30 -IntervalMs 50 -Csv $FE_OUT\frames-positive.csv` in the background; after 3 s, `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts positive` (one default-mode `screenshot` of a background magenta target, 1 s after installing the logger); when the loop has ended, `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts frames $FE_OUT\frames-positive.csv --for positive`. | `fe-positive: human <T#h> (cyan bar); target <T#x> (magenta bar, background, leased to fe-positive)`; `fe-positive: screenshot <T#x> (default mode) → ok; call <t0> → <t1> (<ms> ms)`; `fe-positive: human tab hidden +<a> ms, visible again +<b> ms`; `fe-positive: POSITIVE (page timestamps) PASS — the human's-tab logger saw hidden → visible`. Then `fe-frames: <n> frames; frames showing magenta <m>, cyan <c>` and `fe-frames: POSITIVE (capture loop) PASS — a frame during the flash shows the target's magenta bar; none before` (rule: ≥ 1 frame in [`<t0>`, `<t1>` + 500 ms] shows magenta, and ≥ 1 frame in the 3 s before `<t0>`, none of them magenta). **If the page part FAILs**, FE-4's page-timestamp clause is not discriminating: recorded, and **the run stops and escalates** (no FE-1…FE-3). **If the capture part FAILs**, FE-4's "no window capture shows the target" clause is void: recorded, **the run stops and escalates** — the clause is never silently dropped. |
| FE-1 ★ | BG-1 with `focusEmulation:true`: trusted click into a background tab | Capture loop running for the block (wave sheet). **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts fe1` — three trials, each on a fresh tab. | Per trial `k`: `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: human <T#h>; target <T#x> (background, leased to fe-1); target visibilityState hidden`; `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: click e<N> trusted focusEmulation:true → ok {"ok":true,"focusEmulation":"on"} (<ms> ms)`; `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: target status "status: TRUSTED click received"; field value ""; target visibilityState hidden (read after the call returned)`; `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: human's side — listTabs reads <n> polls (<m> overlapping the call) + before/after; human current, never hidden; window active tabs unchanged; target never active → FE-4 trial PASS`; `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: observation — target page visible +<v> ms, hidden again +<h> ms (start hidden; read after the call hidden)`; `fe-1: FE-1 trial <k>: PASS`. Row: `fe-1: FE-1 PASS — 3/3 trials PASS; FE-4 trials 3/3 PASS (the window clauses: frames --for block)`. **PASS rule per trial** = BG-1's (§4.14), copied, with the option added: the call returns without error and its result carries `focusEmulation: "on"`; the status line exactly `status: TRUSTED click received`; the target's `visibilityState` `hidden` **as read after the call returned** (#78 item 1); the human's tab not `hidden` from 50 ms before the call; afterwards the human's tab active in its window and `current`, the target in the background. A failing trial prints `FAIL — <the clauses that failed>`. FE-1 PASSes only if **all three** trials PASS. |
| FE-2 ★ | BG-2 with `focusEmulation:true`: trusted typing into a background tab | As FE-1: `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts fe2`. | As FE-1 with `fe-2`/`FE-2`, the call line `type e<N> "bg2typed" trusted focusEmulation:true → ok {"ok":true,"focusEmulation":"on"} (<ms> ms)` and the status line `target status "status: TRUSTED input = bg2typed"; field value "bg2typed"; target visibilityState hidden (read after the call returned)`. **PASS rule per trial** = BG-2's (§4.14), copied, with the option added: as FE-1's, with the status line exactly `status: TRUSTED input = bg2typed` **and** the field value exactly `bg2typed`. All three trials must PASS. (The extension focuses the field inside the debugger session, after the enable, #85 item 3.) |
| FE-3 ★ | Trusted `pressKey` with `focusEmulation:true` into the background tab's trusted-only input | As FE-1: `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts fe3`. **Focus step** (in the same trial, before the pressKey): `evaluate` of `el.focus()` on `#trusted-input` (the bridge's `evaluate`, its own debugger session, ended before the pressKey's), which returns whether `document.activeElement` is then `#trusted-input`. **Key**: `ArrowLeft` (`keys.ts` sends it with `windowsVirtualKeyCode` 37 and no `text`; in an empty field it changes no value, so no `input` event overwrites the keydown's status). | As FE-1 with `fe-3`/`FE-3`; the first line ends `; focus step (evaluate el.focus()): document.activeElement is #trusted-input: yes`; the call line `pressKey ArrowLeft trusted focusEmulation:true → ok {"ok":true,"focusEmulation":"on"} (<ms> ms)`; the status line `target status "status: TRUSTED key = ArrowLeft"; field value ""; target visibilityState hidden (read after the call returned)`. **PASS rule per trial** = PASS = `status: TRUSTED key = ArrowLeft`, with FE-1's other clauses (no error, `focusEmulation: "on"`, target `hidden` after the call, the human's tab and the target afterwards). A focus step that prints `no` is recorded; the trial is judged by the same rule (it FAILs if the key did not arrive). All three trials must PASS. *Whether `el.focus()` sets `document.activeElement` in a hidden tab is unmeasured; the line records it.* |
| FE-4 ★ | The human's side over **all** trials of FE-1…FE-3 (#78 item 1) | The capture loop runs from before FE-1 to after FE-3 (`-StopFile`), Wf in the foreground (`Set-T5Foreground` → `True` before the loop starts). **[SH]** after FE-3: `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts frames $FE_OUT\frames-block.csv --for block`; `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts verdict`. | Per trial (FE-1…FE-3 lines above): `… → FE-4 trial PASS`: in every `listTabs` read (before, each poll, after) the human's tab is `current`; every window's active tab is the one it was before the call; the target is never `active`; the human's tab's logger shows no `hidden` at any time since it was installed for that trial (stricter than BG's "from the call"); a failed poll fails the trial. Block: `fe-frames: FE-4 (window capture over 9 trials, <n> frames) PASS — no frame shows the target; the foreground window never changed` — no frame from 500 ms before the first call to 1500 ms after the last shows the target's magenta bar, and the foreground window's handle is the same in every frame of that span. The block capture is **void** (⇒ escalate, as the positive control) unless every call has a frame within 1000 ms before and after it and no gap over 1000 ms around it. Verdict line: `fe-verdict: FE-4: trials 9/9 PASS; window capture PASS (no frame shows the target; foreground constant) → PASS`. **The debugging banner is expected and is not a FAIL.** **Observation, not a PASS clause:** the target's own page visibility (each trial's `observation —` line; expected by Chromium's source: `visible` while the option is on, `hidden` again after the call). |
| FE-3c | *Extra observation, not in the outcome table (proposed by the FE builder):* does FE-3 discriminate? FE-3 without the option | After FE-5 last. **[SH]** `npx tsx recipes/fe.ts fe3 --control` (same focus step and key, no `focusEmulation`). | Per trial as FE-3 without `focusEmulation:true`; finally `fe-3c: FE-3c observation (not in the outcome table) — without the option the key reached the page in <n>/3 trials`. Recorded either way. If `<n>` > 0, FE-3's PASS says nothing about emulation — recorded and listed for the user; the table is still read as written. |

**The outcome table** (copied verbatim from kickoff §7 / phase doc Stage FE, with #78's precedence;
not changed here). The session agent records the matching decision, "The background-input policy",
citing the rows, and S3 sets `sdk/src/policy.ts` from it:
- **(A) FE-1, FE-2, FE-3 PASS in all trials and FE-4 PASS** → policy `"focusEmulation"`: the SDK's
  trusted actions send `focusEmulation: true`; no `focus()`, no flash; the MCP's trusted tools do the
  same (S5).
- **(B) FE-1 and FE-2 FAIL, or FE-4 FAIL** (the human saw something) → policy `"focusFirst"`: the
  plan's fallback (0.4; #66 "Revisit if") — `Tab.click/type/pressKey({trusted:true})` call `focus()`
  first, and the docs say the tab comes to the front and stays there.
- **(C) mixed** (one input kind passes all trials, another fails; FE-4 PASS) → per kind: the passing
  kinds use emulation, the failing kinds `focus()` first; recorded per kind.
- A trial that passes once and fails once counts as FAIL for that kind (no best-of-three).
- FE-5 passing (the control no longer fails) voids the comparison: record it, read the policy as (B),
  and list it for me.
- **Precedence (#78 item 2):** FE-5 void → (B); else FE-4 FAIL → (B); else every kind passes all trials
  → (A); else no kind passes → (B); else (C). FE-0 FAIL is "stop, reload, re-check", not an outcome.
  FE-5 "passes" if the control receives input in any trial; FE-5 runs first and last. Each trial uses a
  fresh fixture tab or a reset status line. FE-4 is judged over all trials of FE-1…3.
- *#78 item (4), verbatim, for S3:* "(4) Under (A)/(C), the SDK sends `focusEmulation: true` on every
  trusted-capable click/type (it cannot see the extension's automatic escalation); (C) is a per-kind map
  in `policy.ts`; `type {submit}` takes the stricter of type and pressKey. Under (B), the MCP's trusted
  tools keep today's behaviour (no automatic focus; `browser_focus_tab` stays an explicit call) and only
  the SDK's `Tab` focuses first."

`fe.ts verdict` applies exactly this, after three gates that are not outcomes (each prints `STOP — …`
and exits 1): the last FE-0 record PASSes and precedes every trial; the positive control's two parts
PASS; the block capture is not void. Every recorded trial counts — a re-run adds trials, it never
replaces them — and fewer than three recorded trials for a row (or for an FE-5 block and kind) prints
`INCOMPLETE — … no outcome`. Its last line is one of `OUTCOME (A) — …`, `OUTCOME (B) — FE-5 void: …`,
`OUTCOME (B) — FE-4 FAIL: …`, `OUTCOME (B) — no kind passes: …`, `OUTCOME (C) — per kind: click <p>,
type <p>, pressKey <p>; type {submit} takes the stricter of type and pressKey: <p> (#78 item 4)`.

**Evidence rules.** Fixture pages only (the repo is public, B2): the run record copies the scripts'
lines; `fe-results.jsonl` and the two frames CSVs carry no URL, title or image and are attached as
`FE-run-results.txt`, `FE-frames-positive.txt`, `FE-frames-block.txt`; no window image is kept. If any
output shows a non-fixture URL, stop the row, record only "non-fixture tab touched — row invalid".
**Log hygiene** at the end: §4.12's block (copy `daemon.log`, count `://`, the token and withheld lines
without printing them); expected `0`, `0`, `0`, else the log is not attached; attached as
`FE-daemon-log.txt` (`*.log` is gitignored).

driver window `…?t5=<marker>` (`Open-T5Window`); `<T#…>` are handles.*

**Tools** (`recipes/`, written before the run; `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s6.json` exit 0;
their rules are tested by `npx vitest run recipes/`): `sdk-login.ts` (SDK-1), `s6.ts <command>` (the
stale check, SDK-2…5, SM-0, SM-1 (`sm1`, `sm1-log`), the helpers `peek`, `daemon-shutdown` and `close-bb-tab`),
`blackboard/boot-bb.ts` (SDK-6), `blackboard/export-grades.ts` + `blackboard/assemble-mcp.ts` (SDK-7);

`pp1` course key added 2026-10-23 by the S6 fixer r1, before any run.)* Run each with
`npm run recipe -- <file> [args]`. Exit 0 = PASS, 1 = FAIL or a bridge error (`FAILED — …`), 2 = set-up
or refusal, 3 = not measured.

(b) the recipe's `snapshot {tab: T<a>}` after the restart → `TAB_GONE`; (c) **no handle minted before the quit resolves after it:** `listTabs` after the restart lists no handle ≤ `T<m>`, the highest handle in `daemon.status` before the quit (the recipe already prints both) — no call is made on any other pre-quit handle, since a non-fixture tab is never snapshotted or evaluated; (d) unchanged: no tab owned by the recipe, the daemon pid unchanged (`Get-BridgeDaemon` before = after), the same daemon build (12 hex before = after), `reconnected: no` (and an uptime that kept counting), the counts equal after `-CloseBlank` (and equal to the count before step 2 after step 12); FAIL on any live `T<a>` after the restart (a snapshot that answers, or `T<a>` listed); (e) the log after `$Q` (read from `$Q` truncated to its whole second, as the log stamps whole seconds: a line in that second counts, so it can only make the count FAIL; S6 fixer r3) has **exactly one** `extension session changed: <n> handles retired` line (any `<n>` ≥ 0; `<n>` recorded), nothing else on it (no marker value), and an `extension resync: <t> tabs, <g> gone` line at most 30 s after it — stricter than #99's minimum, written now (step agent ruling). Also FAIL (kept from the Part 2 rule): two `tabGone` for `T<a>`, or one with another `lastUrl`. Rule: `sm1Part3Verdict` (`recipes/s6-rules.ts`), dry-run by `recipes/test/s6-rules.test.ts`, where Part 2's measured lines (tabGone at the quit, 0 retired) pass and no-tabGone-and-0-retired, a pre-quit handle listed, a snapshot that answers, two session-changed lines and none each fail. **Procedure changes** (steps (4) and (10)): `npm run recipe -- recipes/s6.ts sm1 --observed <S6_OUT>\sm1-observed.json` (an absolute path outside the repo, not existing yet); it prints the lines above up to `daemon.status after: …`, then `observations written to <S6_OUT>\sm1-observed.json (held tab's events, handles, counts; no URL or title)` and `the verdict is sm1-log's "SM-1 (Part 3 rule)" line (run sm1-log --since $Q --observed <this file>)`, exit 0, and **no** `SM-1 PASS/FAIL` line (or `SM-1 (Part 3 rule) NOT MEASURED — the extension did not disconnect and reconnect during the hold — the restart was not seen`, exit 3). Then `npm run recipe -- recipes/s6.ts sm1-log --since $Q --observed <S6_OUT>\sm1-observed.json`: the session lines since `$Q`, any `finding: …` lines (a tabGone outside the window), then `SM-1 (Part 3 rule) PASS — <T#a> gone by tabGone at the quit (+<ms> ms after $Q); snapshot → TAB_GONE; no handle ≤ T<m> listed after the restart; none owned by s6-sm1; same daemon (build <12 hex>…, no reconnect, uptime kept counting); one session-changed line (<n> retired), resync +<s> s` (or `… gone by the session change (<n> retired); …`), exit 0; `SM-1 (Part 3 rule) FAIL — <reasons>`, exit 1; `… NOT MEASURED — …`, exit 3; **no** `SM-1 LOG …` line. The row is PASS when the `SM-1 (Part 3 rule) PASS` line, the pid and the counts all hold. **REP-0 — added 2026-10-23 (S6 resume, #97), before its run.** *Written by S6 builder r2 from the code
(`recipes/s6.ts rep0`, `recipes/s6-rules.ts rep0Verdict`, dry-run by `recipes/test/s6-rules.test.ts`) for
decisions.md · 2026-10-23 · A probe of the failure-report screenshot runs before any row that could attach a
non-fixture tab (#97), on the S6 live verifier's MINOR-1 (`…-steps/S6-live-verifier.md`): SDK-2/SDK-3's reports,
screenshot included, came back 180–190 ms after the call, where a switch capture hid the human's tab for
~1.5 s in Runs 8/9 (R9-SCREEN-1), so the report's capture may not switch and could show the window's visible
tab instead of the failed one. Run order and the gate: `S6-wave-sheet.md` § "Resumed run".*

| ID | Objective | Steps | Expected |
|---|---|---|---|
| REP-0 ★ | The failure report's screenshot switches to the failed (background) tab and shows it, not the tab on screen | Driver window Ws on `…?t5=s6-human`, uncovered, current (the sheet's step 5; B1 → `<T#h> fixture`); `$env:S6_OUT` set (an existing directory outside the repo); no other client holds `<T#h>`. **[SH]** `Rcp recipes/s6.ts rep0` (= `npm run -s recipe -- recipes/s6.ts rep0`). The recipe: sets `BRIDGE_FAILURES_DIR` to a fresh `$env:S6_OUT\rep0-failures-<epoch>` (before it connects; the user's `~\.browser-agent-bridge\failures` is not used); refuses (exit 2, nothing opened) unless the current tab is the `s6-human` fixture; opens its probe tab `…?s6=rep0` in the background and refuses (exit 2) unless it is inactive and in `<T#h>`'s window; paints a blue bar on `<T#h>` and a magenta bar on the probe (t5-lib `barExpression`, by `evaluate` — the CDP banner shows while each runs) and refuses unless `<T#h>` reads `visible` and the probe `hidden`. Then (1) **control A**: a default-mode `screenshot` of `<T#h>` (on screen: no switch), read by the hue rule; (2) **the probe**: page-side `visibilitychange` logs (`VISLOG_INSTALL`) on both tabs, then `click {role:"button", name:"No such button"}` on the probe tab through the SDK with failure reports on, timed `t0`/`t1`; 2.5 s later both logs are read; the report PNG is read, judged and deleted at once; (3) **control B** (the positive control, after the probe so that the report's capture is the first switch to a freshly opened background tab, as in SDK-2/SDK-3, with no earlier restore in flight): fresh logs, then an explicit default-mode `tab.screenshot()` of the same background probe tab (known to switch, R9-SCREEN-1), timed `c0`/`c1`, read the same way; the control images are kept in memory only. Finally it closes its probe tab and empties and removes its failures directory. **[SH]** the failures-folder rule around it (`$F0`/`$F1` on `$Fail`: 0 new files expected, since the report goes to `$env:S6_OUT`). | `<ts> s6-rep0: ` opens each line. `connected — daemon build <sha>, extension connected; failure reports on, BRIDGE_FAILURES_DIR = <S6_OUT>\rep0-failures-<epoch>`; `opened <T#p> (background) at http://localhost:8080/e2e-playground.html?s6=rep0`; `human <T#h> (?t5=s6-human, current); probe <T#p> (background, same window, leased to s6-rep0)`; `bars: human <T#h> blue (visibilityState visible); probe <T#p> magenta (visibilityState hidden)`; `control A (the human tab <T#h>, on screen): <n> bytes, <w>x<h>, bar probes (<x1>,24) #<hex> (<x2>,24) #<hex> (<x3>,24) #<hex> → blue (hue rule) (expected blue; kept in memory only)`; `click {role:"button", name:"No such button"} → LOCATOR_NOT_FOUND: no button named "No such button".<…> (<ms> ms, report included)`; `screenshotPath: <S6_OUT>\rep0-failures-<epoch>\<ISO with :/. as ->-<T#p>.png; attachErrors: []`; `report PNG: <n> bytes, <w>x<h>, bar probes … → magenta (hue rule) (expected magenta); deleted: yes`; `report call: probe <T#p> visible +<a> ms, hidden again +<b> ms; human <T#h> hidden +<c> ms, visible again +<d> ms → flash <f> ms (informational; call <ms> ms)`; `after the report: human active, current; probe background (informational)`; `control B (tab.screenshot() of the background probe <T#p>): <n> bytes, … → magenta (hue rule) (expected magenta; kept in memory only)`; `control B call: <ms> ms; probe visible +<a'> ms; human hidden +<c'> ms, visible again +<d'> ms (informational)`; `REP-0 PASS — the report's capture switched to the failed tab (visible +<a> ms inside the <ms> ms call) and its PNG shows the probe's magenta bar; controls: the explicit screenshot switched and read magenta, the human tab read blue`; `closed <T#p> (the probe tab)`; `failures directory: 0 file(s) deleted, directory removed`; exit 0. **Rule** (`rep0Verdict`, written before the run): **PASS** = the probe tab logged a `visible` *change* inside [`t0` − 50 ms, `t1` + 50 ms] (the 50 ms clock slack is t5.ts screen1's, Runs 8/9) **and** the report PNG reads magenta by the hue rule. **FAIL** (exit 1, `REP-0 FAIL — <reasons>`) = no such `visible` change (the capture did not switch; a change outside the window is named with its offset), or the PNG reads anything but magenta (blue is named as "the human's tab, not the failed one"; also `mixed`, `other`, unreadable), or no screenshot (no `screenshotPath`, a `screenshot:` line in `attachErrors`, or a PNG that was not read), or a trigger other than `LOCATOR_NOT_FOUND`. **NOT DISCRIMINATING** (exit 3, `REP-0 NOT DISCRIMINATING — <reasons>`) = a control failed — control B did not log a probe `visible` change inside [`c0` − 50, `c1` + 50] ms, its PNG did not read magenta, or it errored; control A did not read blue or errored — or the probe was not `hidden` when its log was installed; it outranks FAIL, and the probe's own reasons follow as `also: …`. The step agent treats NOT DISCRIMINATING as FAIL for the gate and escalates. Set-up refusals are exit 2 (`FAILED — SET-UP: …`): nothing measured, fix and rerun (both recorded). Only a verdict line (`REP-0 PASS`, `REP-0 FAIL` or `REP-0 NOT DISCRIMINATING`) is a result: a `FAILED — …` line with no such verdict line (exit 1 or 2; a set-up or bridge error before the verdict) measured nothing, and the row is rerun after the set-up is fixed, both runs recorded. *(2026-10-23, S6 fixer 2, verifier r2 MINOR-3, before any REP-0 run: an exit 1 with no verdict line was indistinguishable by exit code from `REP-0 FAIL`.)* **Informational, not in the rule:** the report call's duration, the human tab's hidden → visible offsets (the flash), the state after the report. **Method:** Run 9's — the hue rule on three probes of the 48-px bar off its label (`t5-png.ts describeBar`; colour-managed captures read `#dd00fa` for magenta) and page-side `visibilitychange` timestamps (`performance.timeOrigin + performance.now()`) on the probe tab and on the human fixture tab. *2026-10-23 (S6 resume, verifier r2 MAJOR-1, before any REP-0 run): the human tab's colour was cyan, changed to blue because default-mode captures shift cyan/lime's red channel past the hue rule (Run 9 lime → #8fff3d; CDP cyan → #97fdfd) and blue read correctly in Run 9.* **Gate:** on REP-0 FAIL or NOT DISCRIMINATING the rows whose failure report could attach a non-fixture page are **not run** and are recorded "not run — gated by REP-0", and the failure returns as a proposed decision (a code fix in a later stage, never in the wave): **REP-1** (#97 names it; its report is designed to attach while another tab is on screen), **TAB-3** (its session's tab is `https://example.com`, a non-fixture page: an attach-set failure of its `browser_snapshot` would report on it), and **SDK-7's MCP leg** with S6.3's byte-compare (actor M's `browser_evaluate` on the Blackboard course tab: a `PAGE_ERROR` or `TIMEOUT` there is reported, with an image, by the MCP's `failureReport`, `mcp/src/tools/registry.ts:216-238`). Every other remaining row still runs: its reports can only name a fixture tab (the failed tab is a fixture, and a capture that did not switch shows the visible tab of a driver window), or it takes no report at all — the SDK legs of SDK-6/SDK-7 (`failureReports: false`, `recipes/blackboard/boot-bb.ts:41`, `export-grades.ts:106`), `s6.ts close-bb-tab` (reports off since S6 builder r2), and the raw-call recipes `t5.ts` (SCREEN-1), `s6.ts sm1`, `hello.ts` (MUX-8). Per-row reasons: `…-steps/S6-builder-r2.md` §3. |

### 4.20 SAFE rows (Step 9: the kill switch, the JavaScript switch, blocked sites)

*Corrected 2026-10-27: SAFE-5b and SAFE-5c expect the redirect hop to be requested (1 hit on the blocked server); as
built, Step 11b's rule stops it, so a re-run on the Step 11b build expects 0 hits (Run 17 RD-1, RD-3; Step 11b phase
doc § As built). The rows stay as Run 13 ran them.*

*Added 2026-10-24 by Step 9 stage S4's prep builder, before the Step 9 build was loaded and before any SAFE row
ran (exit check S4.1: this commit precedes the build in `git log`). Sources merged, IDs kept:
`docs/plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/stage0-audit.md` §7 (SAFE-0…SAFE-11 with 1b, 4b, 5b–5d, 6b, 6c,
7b–7d), `S2a.md` §7 (rows 1–11 → SAFE-0, SAFE-ACL-1, SAFE-4, SAFE-POP-1, SAFE-CAP-1, SAFE-4c, SAFE-PERF,
SAFE-REL-1…3), `S2b.md` §7 (UI rows 1–8 → SAFE-1…3, SAFE-6, SAFE-OPT-1, SAFE-OPT-2, SAFE-9, SAFE-UNIT, SAFE-PIN),
`S2c.md` §7, `S3.md` §6–§7 (the `Safety:` line from a live transcript, the MCP refusal lines, `daemon.status`
while disarmed, the generation-count delay), and Stage 0's four Chrome-only facts (SAFE-2's key and keyboard
layout, SAFE-WAKE, SAFE-7c, SAFE-6c). New IDs are marked "(new)". Every expected text below is copied from the
committed code at `3821d0f` — `extension/src/safety.ts` (`*_MESSAGE`), `extension/src/badge.ts` (`BADGE_TITLES`,
the press log lines), `extension/src/options-model.ts`, `extension/options.html`, `extension/src/tabs.ts`
(`CAPTURE_MOVED_MESSAGE`), `sdk/src/error.ts` (`safetyRefusalLine`), `mcp/src/tools/registry.ts`
(`safetyStatusLine`, `hiddenLabel`), `daemon/src/intercept.ts` (`safetyLogLine`) — and from the rulings

with its source (a drift test), so a wording change after this commit fails `npm test`, never silently a row.*

*How the wave runs: [`S4-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/S4-wave-sheet.md) (order,
commands, the record template); tags and cautions A1–A10 (§4.12) and B1–B6 (§4.14) hold. Tools (written before
the run, typechecked with `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s9.json`, their rules tested by `npm test`):
`recipes/s9.ts` (SDK rows; every call printed as `ROW <id> <step> <ok|CODE> <message> [data <json>] (<ms> ms)` and
judged as `CHECK <id> <step> PASS|FAIL|NOT DISCRIMINATING — …`), `recipes/s9-actor.ts` (headless `claude -p --model
sonnet` MCP actors with an MCP config naming only `browser-agent-bridge`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--allowedTools
mcp__browser-agent-bridge`; tool results printed item by item), `recipes/s9-win.ps1` (UI Automation in the driver's
own windows: the toolbar button, the shortcut, the options page, `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`, the worker's
console), `recipes/s9-redirect.py` (port 8083: every GET → 302 to the same path on 8081; 8082 is held by a
Windows process, S4 ledger). Fixtures: `python -m http.server` on 8080 **and** 8081, both `--directory
test-fixtures` (the 8081 copy is the blocked origin); test patterns are fixture or reserved names only:
`localhost:8081`, `bank.example`, and the invalid line `not a host!`.*

*Windows and tabs (all the driver's own; the user's windows and tabs are never touched, named or captured):
**W** — `chrome.exe --new-window http://localhost:8080/e2e-playground.html?s9=w`, found by HWND diff; its tab
(`<T#w>`, retitled `S9 W human tab` by `s9.ts setup`) is the human's tab while W is the last-focused normal window;
**O** — the options page, opened by typing `chrome-extension://<id>/options.html` into the omnibox of a fresh
`about:blank` window; **E** — `chrome://extensions/shortcuts`, then `chrome://extensions` (the worker's DevTools
**D** opens from its card); the SDK's own background tabs `a`, `c` (8081), `b5`, `t4` (`s9.ts setup`, in W).
`<id>` is `cpnemjbgecfbeicldpaaegbhjgjgjfep` (§1 item 9). Actor **M** is one persistent MCP actor, started before
SAFE-1 and ended right after its SAFE-5 leg (its lease on `<T#c>` must end before SAFE-5d); a fresh actor **M2**
serves SAFE-9 and is ended at the teardown; each cursor is claimed by its first untargeted tool (Step 8 S5).
(*Amended 2026-10-24, prep fixer r1: was "ended at the teardown", S4-prep-verifier finding 2.*)*

*Pass rules, written now: a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text matches
character for character (the live verifier compares the record with this table row by row, S4.3); "record, not
a check" rows pass by being recorded; "not measured" and "unit-tested only" rows are listed so the record is
complete and never count as a pass. **An `ok` is not evidence**: every row reads a refusal's code and exact
message, a page reporter (the counter button's name `Click counter: N`, the `#trusted-input` length, a
`visibilitychange` log), a UIA read, or a transcript line. A failed row is recorded, never fixed in the wave. The
safety state is restored to the state found before the next row, except where rows chain (said in the row).*

**The found state and the restore (S4.4).** No safety key has ever been written in this profile (the extension in
Chrome runs a pre-Step-9 build, S4 ledger), and `parseStoredSafety` (`extension/src/safety-state.ts`) reads absent
keys as the defaults `{armed: true, evaluate: true, blockList: ""}` (#171). So the found state is expected to read
`safety armed=true evaluate=true blockedCount=0` (`daemon.status`) and, on O, `armed=true evaluate=true count=0
lines=0 sha256=e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855` (the sha256 of the empty text)
with the status line `Bridge armed.`. ~~Whatever is found is what SAFE-11 restores; a non-empty block list is saved
by `Read-S9SafetyState -SaveTo` to `…\scratchpad\s4-found\` outside the repo (never printed, never cat'd) and
deleted after the read-back matches.~~ *Superseded 2026-10-24 (S4 prep fixer r1, before any row ran): was "whatever
is found is what SAFE-11 restores", with a non-empty found list saved to the scratchpad, because every toggle in
the wave is a blind press and the restore targets the default (S4-prep-verifier finding 4).* **Guard:** a found
state other than armed + JavaScript on + an empty list (on O, read from a fresh page, or in `daemon.status`) stops
the wave before any toggle (`Test-S9FoundState`); nothing is changed and the user's list is never copied. After the
wave the three keys exist with the found values: absent and
present-with-default read identically (`parseStoredSafety`); the keys cannot be removed (the writers test refuses
`storage.local.remove`, #188), so the restore is judged on the read values and the hash (S4.4), not on key
presence. **Every read-back is of the stored state** (`Read-S9StoredState`: O is sent to `about:blank` and to a
fresh options page, which fills its controls from `storage.local`; the read waits until `#safety-status` is
rendered), cross-checked with `daemon.status` (`s9.ts wait-safety`). **On any stop or helper throw** from the
driver's first window on, the wave runs `Restore-S9All` (`recipes/s9-win.ps1`: the safety restore and its
read-back, then the pin state, the keyboard layout and the driver windows); a restore that does not read back
equal is a STOP that escalates (G9-1) — the wave sheet's stop rule.

*Note 2026-10-24 (after Run 13): SAFE-9's last clause expects the daemon's `safety state: …` line after the reload's
`extension resync` line; the daemon writes it one line before, by design — `daemon/src/intercept.ts:278` logs the safety
line when the hello's `safetyState` arrives, while `:243` logs `extension resync` only after its `listTabs` returns (Run
13: both at 14:31:26Z, the safety line first; SAFE-0's at 14:13:39Z the same way). The row is not edited after its run:
that clause stays **FAIL (expected text)** for Run 13; a later wave's copy of the row should expect it before ([`S4-live-verifier.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/S4-live-verifier.md) finding 4).*

*Note 2026-10-24 (after Run 13): SAFE-10 (console set-up) expects the worker console window titled with the extension's
own URL; Chrome 156 titles the service worker's DevTools window just `DevTools` (8 characters). That the window is the
worker's console is shown by its content (SAFE-10's console half, 27 `safety: disarmed by the user` hits). The row is not
edited after its run: the set-up row stays **FAIL (expected text)** for Run 13 ([`S4-live-verifier.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step9-phase-e-safety-steps/S4-live-verifier.md) finding 3).*

### 4.21 R14 rows (Step 10 S1, Run 14: Step 9's live follow-ups)

*Corrected 2026-10-27: R14-5c expects the 8081 target logged once, "since the hop is still requested"; as built,
Step 11b's rule stops the hop, so a re-run on the Step 11b build expects 0 hits (Run 17 RD-3; Step 11b phase doc § As
built). The row stays as Run 14 ran it.*

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 10 stage S1's prep builder, before any R14 row ran, on the build already on disk (Step 9's
code; daemon build `99bb55c5…`; no `npm run build` in this wave — phase doc 0.1). These rows are **re-measurements as new
rows**: §4.20's rows and Run 13's results are never edited (decisions.md · 2026-10-24 · S4: Dated notes on §4.20 for
two expected-text errors (#195)). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups.md` 0.10 (quoted
under each row), decisions.md · 2026-10-24 · S4: SAFE-5c's redirect half, SAFE-POP-1, SAFE-3 and SAFE-4(b) are
re-measured in the next live wave (Step 10) (#194), · S4: SAFE-REL-3 prints a URL class, not "withheld" (#196), · S4:
The redirect server uses port 8083 (#197), · S4: Live runners return on every stop; one stop rule for sheet and brief
(#193); Stage 0 F20 and F22 (`…-steps/stage0-audit.md`). Every expected text below is printed by the committed helpers
and tied to the product's constants by `recipes/test/s9-rules.test.ts` (the drift tests of §4.20 plus the R14 ones:
`BLOCKED_URL_MESSAGE`, the fixture's `Open popup` link, `describeDebuggerError`'s `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`, the extension's
wrapper schemes, the options page's `Bridge armed.`); the event and log ORDER was re-read in the code (#195's lesson:
`daemon/src/intercept.ts` logs `safety state: …` when the extension's `safetyState` arrives, before `extension resync`,
which waits for its `listTabs`).*

*How the wave runs: [`S1-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups-steps/S1-wave-sheet.md) — every block
is one call of `recipes/s9-r14.ps1 -Block <name>` (the wave's globals kept between calls; one stop procedure). Helpers
(written before the run; their rules tested by `npm test`, typechecked by `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s9.json`):
`recipes/s9.ts` (`ROW …`/`CHECK …` lines as in §4.20; Run 14 adds `navigate-class`, `download-file`, `pop14`, `r14-type`,
`redirect-probe`, `server-logs`, `rect-check`, `wait-safety --guard`; every throw is one `STOP: …` line, exit 2),
`recipes/s9-rules.ts` (the pure rules), `recipes/s9-win.ps1` (UI Automation in the driver's own windows; Run 14 adds the
window placement, the 20 s banner poll, UIA-only pinning, `STOP:` lines, the raw output file, a proven fresh options page
for every stored read — S4-live-verifier finding 7), `recipes/s9-redirect.py` (now a `ThreadingHTTPServer` on 8083),
`recipes/s9-r14.ps1` (the block runner). Fixtures: `python -m http.server` on 8080 and 8081 (`--directory
test-fixtures`, 127.0.0.1); the only test pattern is `localhost:8081`. Windows (the driver's own; the user's windows and
tabs are never touched, named or captured): **W** (`…?s9=w`, its tab retitled `S9 W human tab`), **E**
(`chrome://extensions`), **D** (the worker's DevTools, titled `DevTools` on Chrome 156 — printed `<DevTools window>`),
**O** (the options page); the SDK's background tabs `a`, `c` (8081), `b5`, `t4` (`s9.ts setup`), `r3`. No headless MCP
actor runs in this wave.*

*Pass rules, written now (as §4.20): a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text
matches character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed; `…` after a toolbar name = Chrome 156's second
line, compared on the first line only); "RECORD" clauses pass by being recorded and are never judged; `NOT MEASURED`
never counts as a pass. **An `ok` is not evidence**: each row reads a refusal's code and message, the page's own
reporter (`#trusted-input`'s value), a server log count, or a UIA read. A failed row is recorded, never fixed in the
wave. **The toolbar button:** R14-3 (the re-arm click) and R14-4b (the disarm and the re-arm) need the pinned button;
no row uses `Alt+Shift+K` (R14-3's text names "the toolbar click", and in R14-4b the keys would land in a window whose
page is receiving trusted typing); every other change goes through the options page. **Before any state change** the
pin state is read by UIA (block `window`): pinned → go on; not pinned → one UIA-only attempt (no mouse); still not
pinned → `STOP: … HUMAN GATE (pinning) …` and the runner returns (#192/#202 do not pre-approve a mouse click).*

*Amended 2026-10-25 by the S1 prep fixer (round 1), still before any R14 row ran, applying the step agent's rulings on
`…-steps/S1-prep-verifier.md` F1–F8, N1–N5 (report `…-steps/S1-prep-fixer1.md`): SAFE-0's tuple is read before any
reload (F3); R14-REL-3's `not listed` / `hidden=disarmed` are NOT MEASURED (F4); R14-3's guard needs no occluder (F5);
R14-4b has its own type verdict (F6); R14-LOGS judges the console by S1.6 as written (F2); the restore prints the
block-list hash comparison, never a non-empty hash (F7/F8); the teardown closes r3 explicitly (N1); the background
`r14-type` is tracked and ended before the restore on any stop (F1). The block-list COUNT may be printed (the daemon
logs it by design, #132); the sha256 of a non-empty list never is; the empty text's sha256 may be.*

**STOP RULE (#193): on any STOP line — a restore that fails, a guard that prints STOP, a human gate (pinning, a
grant), any throw — run the restore first if the safety state was touched since the found-state record, record its
read-back, write the ledger, and RETURN to the step agent. A helper may be fixed only between rows, before any
observation of the next row, and never past a STOP line.**

| R14-LOGS (S1.6) ★ | `daemon.log` and the worker console: 0 for the token, a non-fixture `://`, and the test patterns, by count | `-Block logs`: `Read-S9WorkerConsole D`; `s9.ts scan-file <console> --mode console-s16`; `s9.ts scan-log --since <wave start> --copy …-steps/S1-daemon-log.txt` | Console: `worker console: <n> texts written to … (not printed; Console tab found: True)`; `SCAN …r14-console.txt: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other 0) token=0 localhost:8081=0 :8083=0 (raw 8083 <n>) bank.example=0`; `CONSOLE safety: …` lines (R14-4b's `safety: disarmed by the user` is the positive control); `CHECK R14-LOGS scan-console PASS — all S1.6 counts 0 (token, non-fixture ://, localhost:8081, :8083, bank.example); positive control: <n> "safety: disarmed by the user" line(s)`. Daemon log: `SCAN daemon.log lines since <iso> (<n> lines): ://=0 (fixture 0, other 0) token=0 localhost:8081=0 :8083=0 (raw 8083 <n>) bank.example=0` + `CHECK SAFE-10 daemon-log PASS — all counts 0` + `SAFE-10: <n> lines written to …S1-daemon-log.txt`. **Pass rule (S1.6 as written):** in both SCAN lines `token=0`, `other 0`, `localhost:8081=0`, `:8083=0`, `bank.example=0`, **whoever wrote the line** — a Chrome-owned console entry naming the test pattern FAILS R14-LOGS too (`s9-rules.ts consoleVerdictS16`, prep fixer r1, F2; Run 13's `consoleVerdict`, which tolerated Chrome's own lines, is not used here); a console that could not be read, or one without the positive control, is NOT MEASURED, never a pass. Both halves are counted first; then a non-zero count in either is `STOP: block logs: R14-LOGS FAILS: …` (the FAIL is recorded, the stop procedure restores; nothing of the capture is committed and a non-zero daemon log is not copied). | `s9.ts` |
| R14-TEARDOWN (framing) | The desk as found; every process of the wave ended | `-Block teardown` (prep fixer r1, N1: r3 closed explicitly, whatever it shows — by `closeTab`, or, when the bridge refuses it, by W's own UIA: `Close-S9OtherTab W 'S9 W human tab'`, the one tab in W's strip not W's own, no title printed; then `cleanup` again) | `cleanup: <c> of <n> opened tabs closed, <g> already gone, <l> still listed` + `cleanup: still listed none` (or `still listed r3=<T#>` → `closed the one tab in window <h> not named 'S9 W human tab…' (title not printed)`, the second `cleanup: still listed none`, `teardown: r3 closed by the driver window's UIA …`; any other tab still listed is a `STOP:` line), so c + g = n accounts for every tab the wave opened; `RESTORE desk: Chrome windows now <n> (found <n>)` (equal); `daemon.shutdown sent`; each server `… safe: the tree of <p> does not contain the driver`; `ports 9234, 8080, 8081, 8083 free; Chrome windows <n> (found <n>); bridge MCP processes 0`; `raw output copied to …S1-run-raw.txt (scan passed)`. A failure is a `STOP:` line. | `s9-r14.ps1` |

*Note 2026-10-25 (S1 wave fixer, after Run 14 attempt 2's Return 2; `…-steps/S1-wave-fixer.md`): a procedure step R14-3
names changed; its expected text and pass rule did not. `-Block r3` now calls `Find-S9FreePlacement` where it called
`Set-S9SideBySide`: it tries W's monitor work area, then each other monitor's work area in the order the system lists
them (`s9.ts placement-order`); each try moves the driver windows E and D inside W's rect (off O's rect), places W and
O side by side, and prints `ROW R14-3 placement-attempt-<n> …` and `PLACEMENT attempt <n> on work area […]: free|not
free — …` (records, no CHECK); the first free try is kept (`PLACEMENT chosen: attempt <n> of <m>`), none free → the
same `STOP: … R14-3 placement guard …`. The guard (`placement-before-switch`, `placement-before-click`) is unchanged;
its ROW line now ends with ` · occluder ids driver:<W|E|D|O>, class=<ClassName> process=<name>, …` when the count is
not 0 (never a title). Attempt 3 does not re-run R14-REL-3 or R14-POP-1 (recorded in attempt 2), and R14-LOGS counts
`daemon.log` from attempt 2's wave mark (the step agent's rulings (a), (b), (d) on Return 2). Round 2 (ruling on
`…-steps/S1-wave-verifier.md` F1): each try's area is the work area with its bottom cut to the top of any shell taskbar
window (`Shell_TrayWnd`, `Shell_SecondaryTrayWnd`) whose rect intersects it, an auto-hidden one's visible strip
included, printed as `PLACEMENT work area […] cut to y=<n> by taskbar class=<class>`; a fixed rule with no margin.
The guard is unchanged: any window above O that overlaps it, a taskbar included, still counts and still STOPs.*

*Note 2026-10-25 (S1 wave fixer round 3, after Run 14 attempt 3's Return 3; `…-steps/S1-wave-fixer.md` § Round 3):
procedure steps changed, expected texts and pass rules did not. R14-3 is recorded NOT MEASURED in Run 14 (attempt 3
observed its disarm half, then stopped at `placement-before-click` on a non-driver Chrome window) and is not re-run;
attempt 4 runs R14-4b, R14-5c-ctl, R14-5c, R14-RESTORE, R14-LOGS and R14-TEARDOWN. Before each guard a transient Chrome
window could trip — the toolbar click's foreground and `FromPoint` checks (R14-4b's press and re-arm), the omnibox
foreground/focus check, the one-new-window check, the layout tap and the window count — the driver parks the mouse
cursor at a fixed point, 4 px right of and below the top-left corner of W's page area, and again after each
`FromPoint`-verified click (`PARK cursor at (x,y) in W's page area (…)`, a record). No guard is loosened. The placement
guard lines also print, when there is an occluder, `OCCLUDER <i> …` (rect, owner and root owner, styles) and
`CURSOR at (x,y)` (diagnostics, never a title).*

### 4.22 R15 rows (Step 10 S7, Run 15: Step 10's own rows)

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 10 stage S7's prep builder, before any R15 row ran and before the Step 10 build was loaded
(phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups.md` "Stage S7", exit checks S7.1–S7.6). These rows measure
Step 10's code: 0.2 (MCP trusted tools focus first, #106, #214), 0.3 (the 800-cap warning, #117, #206, #216), 0.4/0.5
(`saveTo`, #127, #205, #207, #217, #218), 0.8 (SW-1, #209 item 3), 0.9 (bb-login's wait, #215), 0.12 (`JS_DISABLED`,
#202, #219), and Run 14's two follow-ups (R15-POP-2 #211, R15-3 #212). RET-1 (0.7, G10-2) and the pp1 leg (0.5, G10-3)
are **prepared here and NOT run**: the record says `not run — waits for the user` (S7.5). §4.20 and §4.21 are not
edited (their rows and results stand; re-measurements are new rows).*

*Superseded 2026-10-25: was "RET-1 (0.7, G10-2) and the pp1 leg (0.5, G10-3) are prepared here and NOT run: the record
says `not run — waits for the user`", because the user granted both (decisions.md · 2026-10-25 · The user grants
RET-1's Chrome kill (G10-2) and the pp1 leg (G10-3) once for Run 15; the button is already pinned (#221), widened by
#222) and both ran in Run 15 after the teardown: PP1 PASS, RET-1 NOT MEASURED (the relaunch helper failed after the
kill), RET-1R PASS (§5 Run 15). The heading "Prepared, NOT run" over the RET-1/RET-1R/PP1 table below is read with
this note; the rows' text is unchanged.*

*How the wave runs: [`S7-wave-sheet.md`](plans/2026-10-24-step10-step8-follow-ups-steps/S7-wave-sheet.md) — every block
is one call of `recipes/s10-r15.ps1 -Block <name>` (Run 14's model, #213: one stop procedure, a narrow resume). Helpers
(written before the run; rules tested by `npm test` — `recipes/test/s10-rules.test.ts`, `s10-fixtures.test.ts` —
typechecked by `npx tsc --noEmit -p recipes/tsconfig.s10.json`): `recipes/s10.ts` (the SDK side; `ROW …`/`CHECK …`
lines; every throw one `STOP: …` line, exit 2), `recipes/s10-rules.ts` (the pure rules and the expected texts, each tied
to its product constant or source line by the drift tests), `recipes/s10-win.ps1` (dot-sources `s9-win.ps1` unchanged;
adds the R15-3 placement with the row as a parameter, the bb-login window close, the memory sampler),
`recipes/s10-mem.ps1`, `recipes/s10-r15.ps1`; reused unchanged: `recipes/s9.ts` (`safe0`, `call`, `wait-safety`,
`wait-alone`, `probe`, `cleanup`, `daemon-shutdown`, `rect-check`, `placement-order`, `park-point`), `s9-actor.ts` (the
headless MCP actors), `s6.ts rep0` (REP-0), `recipes/bb-login.ps1`. Fixtures: `test-fixtures/s10-mcpf.html` (a page
reporter of every click / input / keydown with `isTrusted`), `s10-cap.html` (exactly 1 000 listable buttons `Cap item
0001…1000`), `s10-save.html` (deterministic values and the page's own `TextEncoder` + `crypto.subtle` sha256), served by
`python -m http.server 8080 --directory test-fixtures` on 127.0.0.1 (also as `saveblk.localhost:8080`, which Chrome
resolves to loopback); `recipes/fixtures/` on **8085** for BBL-1. Windows (the driver's own; the user's windows and
tabs are never touched, named or captured): **W** (`…/e2e-playground.html?s9=w`, retitled `S9 W human tab`), **E**
(`chrome://extensions`), **D** (the worker's DevTools, `<DevTools window>`), **O** (the options page), **R** (REP-0's
own, `?t5=s6-human`, closed after it), bb-login's own windows (closed by handle after each leg). The SDK's background
tabs in W (`s10.ts setup`, opened after the reload): `a` (`…?s9=a`), `mcpf`, `sw` (`s10-mcpf.html`), `cap`, `save`,
`close`, `blk` (`s10-save.html`; `blk` on `saveblk.localhost:8080`), `pop`, `jsd` (`e2e-playground.html`). Headless
MCP actors: `claude -p --model sonnet --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <only browser-agent-bridge> --allowedTools
mcp__browser-agent-bridge` (`recipes/s9-actor.ts`), each prompt built by `s10-rules.ts PROMPTS` and sent with an argument
array (no shell), each naming its `tab` explicitly — an explicit `tab` neither claims the tab nor moves the MCP cursor,
and the setup client's leases end with it, so the actors' tabs are unheld; each actor is ended before the next
client needs its tab (`s9.ts wait-alone`).*

*Pass rules, written now: a row passes when every `CHECK` line it names says `PASS` and every quoted text matches
character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed). "RECORD" clauses pass by being recorded and are never
judged; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass. **An `ok` is never evidence**: each row reads the page's own reporter
(`window.__s10log`, `window.__s10cap`, the field's value), the page's own sha256, the file on disk, a refusal's code /
message / data, a server log count, or a UIA read. A failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; it is never re-run
or re-tuned. **Never printed or committed:** the token; the block-list pattern (`saveblk.localhost` is counted, never
printed by a helper line); a `saveTo` path (printed `<saveTo>`; the files are `%TEMP%\bridge-e2e\s15\sv\s15v-*.txt`,
deleted at teardown); an expression (`<expr>`); any value (hashes, sizes, counts only); a URL or title of the user's
tabs; a screenshot.*

**STOP RULE (#193): on any STOP line — a restore that fails, a guard that prints STOP, a human gate (pinning, a
grant), any throw — run the restore first if the safety state was touched since the found-state record, record its
read-back, write the ledger, and RETURN to the step agent. A helper may be fixed only between rows, before any
observation of the next row, and never past a STOP line.**

*Amended 2026-10-25 by the S7 prep fixer (round 1), before any R15 row ran: every row block builds the page values it
reads at its start, so a resume that skipped an earlier block still measures (C1, M3; `blockDependencyGaps` in
`recipes/test/s10-rules.test.ts`); MCPF-1 runs as three blocks and SAVE-FIX, SAVE-64's MCP leg and SAVE-PRIM as their
own, each block bounded by a budget so that it ends under the tool's 600 s cap (m2; the sheet §1 lists each); the
tracked-file guards ignore only the steps dir (M1); SW-1 checks its tab before each capture (M2); a scan that cannot
find the token is NOT MEASURED (NIT). No pass rule's threshold or verdict text changed: SW-1 gains a per-trial
pre-condition, SAFE-0 a known limit, and the new lines the fixes print are added to the expected text where they
appear; the block numbers below follow the new order.*

The pair discriminates (S5: old 13.2 s → 4, new 16.2 s → 0). | `bb-login.ps1`, `s10.ts`, the 8085 log |

`… OUTPUT 1 item(s)`, `…   [1/1] Clicked button "MCPF target"` / `Typed into css #mcpf-input` / `Pressed Escape`; `CHECK MCPF-1 <kind>-<n>-calls PASS — the actor called exactly [browser_<tool>]`; `CHECK MCPF-1 <kind>-<n>-answer PASS — the exact answer`; press — ≥ 1 trusted `keydown` `Escape`; and the tab's row `active` after. **RECORD:** `vis` at each event; whether the actor read the focus sentence correctly. Known limit (F19): focus and input are two calls; one actor cannot show the race. | `s10.ts`, the page reporter, the actor transcript |

| SW-1 ★ | 0.8 with Stage 0 F3: does `onActivated` end `activateForCapture`'s wait? (a differential row) | **0.8's F3 note, verbatim:** "SW-1 is a differential row: per trial, (i) a default-mode screenshot of a background fixture tab in the human's window and (ii) one of a fixture tab already in front of the same window, ≥ 1.5 s apart, three trials; d = client-measured duration of (i) − that of (ii). d ≥ 950 ms in 3/3 → the wait runs to its bound → S7b first names the mechanism (a top-level listener would touch the 'listeners at the top level of `sw.ts`' invariant and escalates); d ≤ 300 ms in 3/3 → the event ends the wait, recorded, no change, the carried input closes; anything else → undetermined, recorded, no change. The `visibilitychange` stamps stay as a record." `-Block sw1`: W in front, its tab `w` selected, the cursor parked; `s10.ts sw1` (a guard: `w` active and current, `sw` behind it in W — else `STOP:`); per trial the VISLOG installed on `w` and `sw`, 1.6 s, **the pre-check of (i)** (`sw`'s `listTabs` row `active=false`), (i) `screenshot {tab: sw}`, 1.6 s, **the pre-check of (ii)** (`w`'s row `active=true`), (ii) `screenshot {tab: w}`, 1.6 s. *Pre-condition added 2026-10-25 by the S7 prep fixer (verifier M2: the restore after (i) is fire-and-forget, so a missed restore would make (ii) switch too and d ≈ 0): a failed pre-check marks that trial NOT MEASURED with the observed state, its capture is not taken, and it is never re-run.* | `ROW SW-1 set-up w active=true current=true · sw active=false same window yes`; per trial `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> pre-(i) sw active=false (needs active=false) → measured`, `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> pre-(ii) w active=true (needs active=true) → measured`, `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> (i) background ok <ms> ms · (ii) front ok <ms> ms · d=<ms> ms` (a failed pre-check: `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> pre-(<i\|ii>) <tab> active=<b> (needs active=<b>) → NOT MEASURED (never re-run)` and `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> NOT MEASURED (…) · … d=not measured`) and `ROW SW-1 trial-<k> vis (RECORD, ms from (i)'s start) w <state>+<ms> … · sw <state>+<ms> …`; then `ROW SW-1 measured <m>/3 trials (a decided outcome needs 3/3)` and `ROW SW-1 outcome d=[<d1>, <d2>, <d3>] ms → <runs to its bound \| the event ends the wait \| undetermined> (RECORD; the outcome table of 0.8's F3 note)`. **The outcome table is the rule** (`swOutcome`, tested on each branch; its thresholds are 0.8's, unchanged); the outcome is recorded either way (phase doc "Continues anyway"). A failed capture makes its d not measured and the outcome undetermined. The table counts only measured trials and needs 3/3 measured for "runs to its bound" or "the event ends the wait", else "undetermined" (`swPre`, `swMeasured`, tested with a not-measured trial). | `s10.ts` |

| R15-POP-2 ★ | #211: the popup's `openerTabId` class with the opener (a) in front and (b) behind, then `leaseGranted` | #211: "Run 15 adds R15-POP-2: it prints the popup's `openerTabId` class (the held opener, another tab, none) with the opener (a) in front and (b) in the background of its window, then judges `leaseGranted`." `-Block pop2`: `s10.ts pop2 --leg a` (claim `pop`, `focusTab pop`, a guard that it is active, the synthetic click on `Open popup`, ≤ 5 s for `leaseGranted`, the class from the new `listTabs` row's `openerTabId` vs the opener's Chrome id), then `--leg b` (claim `pop`, `focusTab w`, the guard that `pop` is behind, the same) | Per leg: `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-claim ok <T#pop> (<ms> ms)`; `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-opener <T#pop> active=<true\|false> (expected in front\|behind)`; `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-click-synthetic ok (<ms> ms)`; `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-class popup opener class: <held opener\|another tab\|none\|no popup\|several popups> (new tabs <n>; never a URL)`; `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-grant leaseGranted reason opener url-is-popup yes after <ms> ms` or `… no leaseGranted within 5 s`; `ROW R15-POP-2 <leg>-reading <reading>`; the popup closed, the opener released. **Reading, written now** (`pop2Reading`): `held opener` → `CHECK R15-POP-2 <leg>-leaseGranted PASS — reason opener, the popup URL, <ms> ms ≤ 5000 ms` or FAIL; `held opener` + no grant = **#211's Revisit-if: a product defect → a fix stage** (`PRODUCT DEFECT …`); `another tab` / `none` + no grant = RECORD (`Chrome named … : the daemon's rule grants nothing (consistent; Run 14's conjecture)`); a grant for another class = RECORD `unexplained …` (the step agent decides); `no popup` = NOT MEASURED. Known limit: the class is read from `listTabs` after the click; the daemon used the `tabCreated` event's `openerTabId`, which no client sees. | `s10.ts` |
| SAVE-1M ★ | 0.4 / S4 §7: a 1 MiB string via `return`, SDK and MCP | `-Block save-a`: `save-build 1m` (and 4m, surr, json; 4m also on `close` — every row block builds the values it reads at its own start, fixer 1 C1/M3); `save-sdk SAVE-1M 1m` (`Tab.evaluateToFile("return window.__s10v[\"1m\"]", {path})`); actor `save`: `save-mcp SAVE-1M 1m` (one `browser_evaluate {tab, expression, saveTo}`) | `ROW SAVE-BUILD 1m page digest kind string length 1048576 bytes 1048576 sha256 <hex> (built in <ms> ms, on <T#save>)`; SDK: `ROW SAVE-1M sdk ok result {"path":"<saveTo>","bytes":1048576,"kind":"string","sha256":"<hex>"} (path equals the asked path: yes) (<ms> ms)`; `ROW SAVE-1M sdk-file exists=yes part=no bytes=1048576 sha256=<hex>`; `CHECK SAVE-1M sdk PASS — page = file = report: 1048576 bytes, kind string, sha256 equal, no .part`; `CHECK SAVE-1M sdk-result-runs16 PASS — no 16-character run of the value in the SDK's result`. MCP: `ROW SAVE-1M mcp TOOL browser_evaluate {"tab":"<T#save>","expression":"<expr>","saveTo":"<saveTo>"}`; `…   [1/1] Saved <saveTo>` / `…   [1/1] 1048576 bytes · string · sha256 <hex>`; `ROW SAVE-1M mcp answer <n> characters (bound <b>) · isError false`; `CHECK SAVE-1M mcp-answer PASS — the exact answer form, <n> ≤ <b> characters, no 16-character run of the value`; `CHECK SAVE-1M mcp PASS — page = file = report: …`. **Rules:** `saveVerdict` (the page's own `TextEncoder`+`crypto.subtle` sha256 = the file's = the reported; bytes equal; kind; no `.part`) and `answerVerdict` (exactly `Saved <path>\n<bytes> bytes · <kind> · sha256 <hex>`, ≤ min(300, path + 120) characters — S4.1 as amended by F13 — and no 16-character run of the value). | `s10.ts`, the file |

### 4.23 R16 rows (Step 11 S5, Run 16: the upload rows, SAVE-REFUSE-R and SW-2)

*Corrected 2026-10-27: the K-opt rows (K-opt-stand, K-opt-home and their `options-model.ts` lines) are Run 16's texts;
as built, S0F-a refuses a home folder (`UPLOAD_HOME_REASON`, no `UPLOAD_HOME_WARNING`) and the lines moved: the
current texts are §4.24's K17 (Step 11b phase doc § As built S0F-a).*

*Added 2026-10-25 by Step 11 slice S5-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5), before any Run 16 row ran and before the Step 11 build was loaded (exit checks S5.1, R.2). Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-25-step11-file-upload.md` 0.6, 0.12 with its correction (#251), 0.14 (a) and (c), § Stage S5, § Exit checks S5.1–S5.7, § Human gates; decisions.md #243, #245, #246, #251, #254, #262, #266, #267, #268, #271; `docs/plans/2026-10-25-step11-file-upload-steps/S3.md` §7 (twelve items; table at the end). §4.20–§4.22 are not edited; SAVE-REFUSE's and SW-1R's Run 15 records stand. Commands are placeholders (`⟨helper: …⟩`): S5-helpers and S5-driver build them to match this section, never the other way round. Fixtures are `s11-*` pages made by S5-fixtures; the CSP page is served on port 8084 (#251).*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** A row passes when every clause named in its "Pass rule" holds and every quoted text matches character for character (`<…>` = a value recorded as observed). `RECORD` clauses pass by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` never counts as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence.** Every upload row is judged by the page's own reporter (count, names, sizes and SHA-256 of what the page received, in order, and `isTrusted` of each `change` event; its SHA-256 equals the sha256 recorded when the wave made the file) and by a MutationObserver record of `data-__bridge-upload` (added, then removed; carriers left = 0 in the light DOM, every open shadow root and every same-origin frame). **Every refusal row:** the reporter holds 0 files, saw no `change` and no `input` event, and the mark was never set. A failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; it is never re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave (a fix is a proposed decision). Never printed or committed: the token; the text of the block list or the Upload folders box (counts, and the sha256 only inside the restore comparison); a path under the user's profile (printed `<ROOT>\name`, `<OUTSIDE>\name`); the sentinel folder's name; the block alias; an expression or a `saveTo` path; a title or URL of a non-fixture tab; a screenshot. Uploads go only to fixture pages on 127.0.0.1 / `*.localhost` (G11-2).

**STOP rule (#193).** On any STOP line (a failed restore, a guard printing STOP, a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button, a port held by a program the wave did not start (#251), an unreadable file-URL toggle, a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on, a tracked file changed outside the steps dir, any throw after the first state change) the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back, writes the ledger and returns. A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line.

**Slices and order (#271).** Each row's "Live" column says where it runs. **liveA** (no file access needed): reload and loaded-build proof (UP-L1), found state (UP-0), UP-OPT, UP-FA (only when the toggle reads OFF), UP-6, UP-7, SAVE-REFUSE-R, SW-2, then UP-RESTORE and UP-LOGS. **liveB** (upload rows, only with file access on: found ON, or G11-3 granted): UP-0 again (liveB's own found record), ROOT added as UP-OPT does (no new row), UP-1…UP-5, UP-6B, UP-8, UP-9, UP-PATCH, UP-TWICE, UP-RAW, UP-NAV, UP-DEVT, UP-STALE, UP-7J, UP-RACE-A, UP-RACE-B, then UP-RESTORE and UP-LOGS again. Each slice leaves the safety state as found, read back by count and hash (S5.4). Within liveA the order is the row order of the table below where it is liveA.

**G11-3 branch (#245).** UP-0 reads the extension's "Allow access to file URLs" toggle on its details page, read only, in a window the wave opened; the wave never presses or changes it. **ON:** the liveB rows run; UP-FA records `not run — the toggle is on`. **OFF:** UP-FA runs (RECORD); each of these 17 rows is recorded `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3` and liveB does not start: UP-1, UP-2, UP-3, UP-4, UP-5, UP-6B, UP-8, UP-9, UP-PATCH, UP-TWICE, UP-RAW, UP-NAV, UP-DEVT, UP-STALE, UP-7J, UP-RACE-A, UP-RACE-B. UP-9 is on this list only because the phase doc (0.12, as corrected by #251) lists it; its refusal is in fact decided by the gate, before the file-access check, and this sheet does not override the phase doc. These **still run** (their decisive answer comes before the handler's file-access check, or needs no upload): UP-L1, UP-0, UP-OPT, UP-FA, UP-6, UP-7, SAVE-REFUSE-R, SW-2, UP-RESTORE, UP-LOGS. **Unreadable** toggle (no or conflicting stateful element): STOP, nothing changed. If a liveB row answers the file-access refusal while UP-0 read ON, that row is FAIL "UP-0's reading is contradicted"; it is not re-read. The found state is never changed by the wave except as #243 allows (the Upload folders box and the block list, restored exactly).

**Windows and state changes.** Only windows the wave opened (the human's tab is the wave's own `W` tab). State changes go through the options page in the wave's own window or the toolbar button (pinned; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click). The wave records what it finds (armed, JavaScript, block list and Upload folders as counts and sha256, the texts kept in files outside the repo) and restores exactly that; the guard is armed with JavaScript on, any block list and any Upload folders text. Each changing row restores its own change before the next row.

If the call succeeds: NOT MEASURED ("DevTools was not attached"), not a product failure. |
