| R19-ARGS-2 ★ (B2.1; the refusals, with an effect counter at 0) | JS on, armed | On `…/e2e-playground.html?s12=a2`: first a **control** `tab.evaluate("(a) => { window.__s12eff = (window.__s12eff \|\| 0) + 1; return window.__s12eff; }", {args: 1})` (answers `1`); then each refusal with the same effect source: the SDK guard's eight (`{x: undefined}`, a function, a symbol, `10n`, `NaN`, a cycle, a throwing `toJSON`, a string of 1 048 577 characters) and the SDK's ninth (`fn` not a string, with `args`); one raw wire call `evaluate {args: <1 048 577-character string>}` past the SDK (the only guard clause that crosses the wire); one `tab.evaluate("1", {args: 1})` (not a function); `EFF` read after | `ARGSREF …` per case; **exact texts** (spec §10 S2a and S2b notes, `shared/src/protocol.ts` `ARGS_REFUSALS`): `args: the value holds undefined, which JSON cannot carry — use null, or leave the key out`; `args: the value holds a function, which JSON cannot carry — pass plain JSON data`; `args: the value holds a symbol, which JSON cannot carry — pass plain JSON data`; `args: the value holds a BigInt, which JSON cannot carry — pass it as a string or a number`; `args: the value holds NaN or an infinite number, which JSON turns into null — pass null or a string`; `args: the value holds a cycle (an object that contains itself), which JSON cannot carry`; `args: JSON.stringify threw on the value (a throwing toJSON or getter) — pass plain JSON data`; `args: the value is 1048579 bytes as JSON, over the 1 MiB cap (1048576 bytes)` (the string's 1 048 577 characters plus two quotes); the ninth, `args: pass the function source as a string, such as "(a) => a.rows.length" — not a function`; the not-a-function one: `args: with args, expression must be a function source such as "(a) => a.rows.length"; it evaluated to number` (`extension/test/s12-args-call.test.ts:192`). Every one `BAD_PARAMS`, no `data`. `ARGSREF total refusals=11 exact=11 data-none=11 sent=2 effects=0 control=1` (`sent` = calls the actor's own wrapper around `client.call` put on the wire; the raw oversize call and the not-a-function call are the two that go out); `EFF R19-ARGS-2 before=1 after=1` | All 11 exact by `message ===` and `code === BAD_PARAMS`, no `data`; **exactly 2 calls were sent** (`sent=2`: the wire-size one and the not-a-function one, each `sent=1`; the other 9 `sent=0`, stopped in the SDK); **the page's effect counter is unchanged by every refused call** (`before=1 after=1`, control = 1, so the counter works). **Discrimination:** a build whose SDK does not check sends all 9, so `sent` rises above 2; a build that checks nothing at the extension runs the oversize string and moves the counter; a build that echoes the value in a message fails `exact` and the sentinel-free scan of R19-LOGS; `effects` above 0 is a STOP (an `args` call ran that must not). A second control: `(a) => a.n;` (a trailing `;`) answers `PAGE_ERROR`, not the not-a-function text (spec S2a note), recorded as `ANSWER R19-ARGS-2 semicolon PAGE_ERROR` (RECORD). | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-args-2`. The replies are in oldest-first order: entry timestamps non-decreasing (counted, `ordered=yes`). **Discrimination:** a build with no replay (`Runtime.enable` reports nothing before it, C2 would then be the design) prints `entries=0`; a build whose every entry has "no known source" prints `entries=0` (S3a handoff 2); a build keeping `url` or `stack` fails those counts; a build ignoring `limit` or `level` fails the second and third calls. `tab.consoleMessages()`; remove the pattern; `tab.consoleMessages()` again. **Leg b (cross site, out of process).** On `http://localhost:8081/s12-console.html?s12=frameoop` (its frame `http://127.0.0.1:8081/s12-console.html?s12=con` is a different site), no pattern: one `tab.consoleMessages()` | `CON R19-CON-FRAME a-pattern entries=2 console=2 exception=0 sentinel=2 url=0 stack=0 …`; the frame's 11 are the difference); with the pattern gone it holds all 13, the page's 2 and the frame's 10 + 1 (spec §10 S3a note: "the tab's own target only", which holds a same-site frame; Stage 0 C5 measured a same-origin frame read 5 of 5). **Leg b:** only the page's own 2 (`S3a-builder.md` §7 check 4: "An out-of-process frame: not read"; `Stage0-measurer1.md:48` C5: "OOPIF 0 of 5 and same-origin iframe 5 of 5" on the tab session). **Discrimination:** with the pattern set, a build that reads the blocked frame returns 13 (a leak: FAIL, counted, never printed); a build that reads no frames at all passes the first read of leg a, so **the second read of leg a is the control that frames are read**: `a-nopattern entries=2` makes leg a's drop clause **NOT DISCRIMINATING** (either the frame is not read or Chrome put it out of process; recorded, not a product FAIL, and leg b is then recorded without that control); leg b returning 13 means a build that auto-attaches to child targets, which the spec excludes: FAIL. The actor cannot tell whether Chrome put a frame out of process (`Target.getTargets` is refused through `chrome.debugger`, Stage 0 C5), so legs a and b rest on site isolation's site rule and prove it together. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-con-frame` (both legs). Leg `allowed`, with the pattern still set: on `…/s12-console.html?s12=noteaok` (page and script both on `localhost:8081`): `tab.consoleMessages()`; remove the pattern | `CON R19-CON-NOTEA blocked entries=0 … marker=0 url=0 stack=0`; `CON R19-CON-NOTEA allowed entries=2 console=2 exception=0 sentinel=1 url=0 stack=0 … marker=1` where `marker` counts occurrences of `s12-notea` and `s12-notea-long` (blocked leg) or `s12-noteaok` (allowed leg) in the whole reply | **Blocked leg: 0 occurrences of either marker, 0 URLs, 0 stack lines** (the page logs only the two objects, so `entries=0`): an entry whose preview carries a frame of the blocked host, or whose shortened preview ends in `…`, is dropped as an unknown source (`extension/test/s12-console-reduce.test.ts:157`, title: "a preview value V8 shortened (its cut part ends in `…`) is an unknown source and drops its entry"; decision #344 (3)); the nested message never appears. The long case checks F1's shape against real V8 (S3b handoff). **Allowed leg (the S3a handoff's second live row, `S3a.md` §7: "an object holding an Error logs no `at` line"):** the Error entry is **present** (`entries=2`: it and the plain sentinel entry; `marker=1`) with `url=0 stack=0`: the reducer keeps an object that holds an Error from an allowed page as `Object {e: Error: …}` with no stack line (`extension/test/s12-console-reduce.test.ts`, the F1 test, title: "an object whose preview holds an Error's stack — a blocked stack URL drops the entry; an allowed one keeps no `at` line"). **Discrimination:** a build that cuts the preview at its first stack line but trusts the part kept prints the blocked entry (`marker ≥ 1`); one that logs the `Error`'s message whole shows it; one that keeps the stack fails `stack=0`/`url=0`. A build that drops every object holding an Error prints `entries=1 marker=0` on the allowed leg, but so does a V8 cut of the preview (Note A's unmeasured question, `S3a.md:66-68`), and the sheet cannot tell them apart: `entries=1 marker=0` is **RECORD** (with both counts), never a FAIL, and that clause is NOT DISCRIMINATING; `entries=0` (the plain entry missing too) is a FAIL. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-con-notea`. Source: `S3b-builder.md` §7 CON-NOTEA; `S3a.md` §7 (Note A and verifier 1's two); decision #344 |
| R19-CON-BIG ★ (C; a 1 MB message is cut) | JS on, armed | On `…/s12-console.html?s12=big`: `tab.consoleMessages()` | `CON R19-CON-BIG big entries=1 console=1 exception=0 sentinel=0 url=0 stack=0 maxlen=2000 cut=1 uncaught-first=yes` | One entry, `maxlen=2000` (an ASCII string: 1 999 characters and `…`; spec: "`text` at most `CONSOLE_TEXT_MAX` (2 000) characters, a cut text ending with `…`"; `S3a-builder.md` §7 check 6: "comes back as 2000 characters ending with `…`"; `extension/test/s12-console-reduce.test.ts:230`), `cut=1`. **Discrimination:** an uncut build returns 1 000 000 characters; a build cutting at 1 000 prints `maxlen=1000`. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-con-big`. The line form is `<ISO time, UTC> <level> [uncaught] <text>` (`sdk/test/s12-console-format.test.ts:10`, example `2026-10-27T10:00:01.123Z error uncaught Uncaught Error: boom`) | Every line matches the form (`form = lines`), the exception line holds ` uncaught `; `lines` equals the SDK's entries (11), a cap marker only ever of the form `… [truncated: <n> more lines, the newest — narrow with level/limit]` (`sdk/test/s12-console-cap.test.ts:32`), absent for 11 short entries; the banner clause: one `BANNERCALL` window around the `{}` read, under the **Banner rule** (expected `seen`; NOT MEASURED without the idle and the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL; never a STOP). **Existing tools, named:** after the legs above the actor calls five tools that existed before Step 12, each without any new parameter: `browser_snapshot`, `browser_evaluate` `"1 + 1"`, `browser_list_tabs`, `browser_status` and `browser_network_requests`; each answers `ok` in its known form (a non-empty snapshot text, `2`, a tab list holding the page's handle, a status text, a request-log text); any error or a changed form is the STOP `existing-tool-changed` (the tool set is the sheet's choice; the 24 pre-Step-12 names are in `mcp/src/tools/registry.ts`). **Discrimination:** a formatter printing JSON, or a line without the `uncaught` marker, fails `form`; a tool that ignores `level` or `limit` fails those legs. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-con-mcp`. The control is the row's own negative leg: a `seen` there means the sampler cannot discriminate and the row is **NOT MEASURED** (never a product pass or fail), as R18-BANNER-CTL. **Discrimination:** a build that reads without attaching (`none` on the positive leg) is a product change worth recording; a banner still `seen` on the after leg means a session was kept past its bound. **Line forms (part B).** Part A's forms and §4.26's are carried. **New in part B** (the contract to S7-helpers; every value a count, a fixed code or a time; never a text): (14) `DEVPROBE method=<helper's name> answer=<DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS|ok|CODE> ms=<n> attached=<yes|no>`, a **row-less** probe (#351 (3)): the helper opens DevTools into a window X the wave opened on a fixture page, then calls `evaluate "1"` on that tab until it answers `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS` or 15 000 ms pass; `attached=yes` only for `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`; (15) `KEEPSEQ <row> calls=<n> gap-ms=<g> answers=<k>/<n> banner=<continuous|gaps:<m>> end-ms=<e> poll-ms=<p>` (`end-ms` = last reply to the first sampler read of `none`; `poll-ms` the sampler's interval); (16) `KEEPEND <row> leg=<ctl|idle|disarm|block|nav-blocked|nav-own|nav-file|close|replace> since-last-call-ms=<n> end-ms=<e> next=<CODE|ok>` (`ctl` = the chooser refusal that drops the session at once, `idle` = no event; both are KEEP-2's references, KEEP-3 compares against them); (17) `CONKEPT first=<n> second=<n> equal=<yes|no> ordered=<yes|no>`; (18) `SWEEPHELD after-reload=<DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS|ok|CODE> devtools-open=<yes|no> reclaimed=<yes|no>`; (19) `KEEPDT <row> leg=<devtools|cancel|closed> next=<CODE|ok|not-measured>`; (20) `OVERLAP <row> leg=<evaluate|chooser> first=<ok|CODE> second=<ok|CODE> exact=<yes|no>`; (21) `CHOOSE <row> <label> <ok|CODE> exact=<yes|no|n/a> files=<n> change=<n> clicks=<n> bytes-equal=<yes|no|n/a> dialog=<n> ms=<n>` (the page's own reporter `window.__s12ch` for `files`, `change`, `clicks`, `bytes-equal`; `dialog` = native dialogs the UIA read counts during the call; `ms` = the call's start to its reply); (22) `DIALOG <row> leg=<a|b|c> before=<n> after-click=<n> after-close=<n> ms=<n> change=<n>` (UIA counts of native file dialogs owned by the wave's window X; `change` = the page's `change` count after the close); (23) `LATE <row> opener-ms=<4900|5100> opened=<yes|no> dialog=<n>`; (24) `HUMAN <row> received=<call-target|clicked-input|none> dialog=<n> files=<n>`; (25) `CONCAP lines=<k> body-chars=<c> marker=<yes|no> first-index=<i> last-index=<j> sentinel=<s>`; (26) `G2 <row> a-reply=<ms> b-reply=<ms> gap-ms=<n> holder-after-reply-ms=<h> a-effect=<n> b=<ok|CODE>` (times from the driver's one clock; `holder-after-reply-ms` = how long a third client's `daemon.status` polls, every 50 ms, kept reading A's client name after A's reply) and `G2TYPE round=<i> sent=40 got=<n> lost=<l> b-calls=<c> b-ok=<k> b-lock-timeout=<t>`; (27) `VERSIONCH <row> leg=<sdk|mcp|raw|control> code=<VERSION|ok|CODE> hint=<yes|no> files=<n> exact=<yes|no>`; (28) `DAEMONLOG <slice> sentinel=<n> console-text=<n> args-text=<n>` (counts in `daemon.log` since the slice's mark of each probe string; the probe strings are fixture constants passed by argument); (29) `SCANTOOL <slice> tool=<name> sentinel=<n> token=<n> profile=<n> shot-line=<n> image-line=<n>` and `SCAN actors: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other <n>) token=<n> sentinel=<n> profile-other=<n> shot-line=<n> image-line=<n>` (`shot-line` = lines of the MCP failure report that name the saved screenshot, form `Screenshot: …\.browser-agent-bridge\failures\….png` (#349 (a)); `image-line` = the client's own `[Image: source: …]` lines (#349 (b)); `profile-other` = profile-path matches **outside** those two named lines, `profile-all` = `profile-other` + the matches inside them, printed once as `profile-all=<n>`); (30) `DIALOGS <slice> native=<n> devtools=<n> wave-windows-open=<n>` (read by UIA at a slice's end, all expected 0 except the wave's own windows listed; `devtools` counts DevTools windows **other than** the worker-console capture window, which is saved and closed before the read: Capture segments); (31) `STOP <reason>` with `<reason>` one of `attach-after-disarm`, `attach-on-refused-page`, `kept-session-used`, `dialog-not-opened`, `outside-file-reached-page`, `args-ran-js-off`, `existing-tool-changed`, `dialog-left-open`, `devtools-left-open`, `not-restored`, `text-leak`, `old-build-left-loaded`, `throw-after-state`, and `RETURN <seq> after-stop=<reason> restored=<yes|no|n/a> readback=<equal|n/a>`, printed by the runner when it returns (R7.6); (32) `FIX <what>` for a helper fix made between rows. **The #351 (4) helper fixes S7-helpers makes, as contract** (recipes only, no pass rule moves): (a) when `OPT O2 shown=no` the helper also prints `O2 did not show the saved text in <n> ms` (the row's verdict unchanged); (b) the runner prints the final `ROW <id> <verdict>` of every row that has a console clause (R19-ARGS-*, R19-CON-*) **before** R19-LOGS runs, or resolves the `console-clause pending` lines first, so a stop inside R19-LOGS leaves those rows judged (Run 18 printed `ROW R18-UP-L1` and `ROW R18-SAVE-REFUSE` only after R18-LOGS, #349); (c) the five preflight rows (R19-0's `MANIFEST`/`RULES`/`STATE`/`TOGGLE`/`FOUNDLIST`) print once per slice, so the aggregator prints no `WARN` about `RULES`. Texts the sheet cannot yet quote are named in **Open items**.

**Banner rule and the line forms added by fix round 1.** No attach counter exists and none is built (#351 (2)). The banner sampler is the only live signal, and `recipes/CLAUDE.md` records its limit: "the banner sampler read positive on legs that send no call (R17B-UP-6 `missing`/`relative`), so a positive reading does not show that the call raised the banner" (Run 17b); Run 18 read `seen` on all five R18-UP-6 legs while its control passed (#351 (2)). So **a banner positive on a refused call is a STOP only when both hold: `IDLE >= 20 000 ms` was printed before the call (since the last reply of any call other than `snapshot`, `listTabs` and `daemon.status`; worst case the keeper's 5 s plus Chrome's 14 s, Open item 8), and the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL passed; otherwise it is `NOT MEASURED`, recorded with both values.** The refused calls inside one row are not CDP calls on a correct build, so the row's one idle covers them. A positive that survives both conditions can still come from Chrome or the sampler; the STOP returns for the session agent to judge and is not a product FAIL by itself. **Where no clean window can be made, the row carries no banner STOP:** R19-KEEP-3 and R19-CHOOSE-DISARM (the refused call follows a CDP call of the same row by seconds), and R19-KEEP-1's banner clause is `NOT DISCRIMINATING`; the live proof of "no attach after a disarm" there is the answers and the unit tests, a gap recorded as Open item 5. **New forms** (counts and fixed words only): (33) `CLOSERPROBE method=<helper's name> opened=<yes|no> closed=<yes|no|n/a> ms=<n> native-after=<n>` (R19-CLOSER); (34) `BANNERCALL <row> leg=<label> idle-ms=<n> polls=<n> banner=<seen|none> control=<pass|fail|absent>` (one per banner-judged window, with the sampler of R19-CON-BANNER's form: 500 ms before to 2 500 ms after the reply; `control` = the slice's R19-BANNER-CTL); (35) `CAPSEG <slice> seg=<k> saved=<yes|no> before=<reload|end>` (Capture segments). **Fix round 2: one IDLE, the R19-0 re-runs, and absent keys.** (a) **IDLE has one definition, used by every row and helper: the time since the last reply of any call other than `snapshot`, `listTabs` and `daemon.status`.** The sheet lists no CDP calls and the helper needs no list: this superset counts every call that can attach. The source is the phase doc's § 0.6: "the first CDP call attaches, later calls reuse it" and "`withDebugger` and every caller (`extension/src/debugger.ts`) go through it". Every "since the last CDP call", "since the control" and "since the last reply of any CDP call" below means this IDLE. (b) **An R19-0 re-run prints only `LOADED rerun=<k> …` (k = 1, 2, …): no `ROW R19-0` line and no preflight lines**, so each id prints exactly one `ROW` per slice; form (1)'s `LOADED` line carries the optional `rerun=<k>` key first. (c) **A key left out of an expected `CON` line means "not judged"**: the keys `ordered`, `newest` and `marker` are judged only on the rows and legs that write them.

**Part B's fixture pages and ports** (described for S7-helpers, none built here; same one server on 8081, §4.27's ports line holds): (4) `test-fixtures/s12-chooser.html` + `s12-chooser.js`, query `s12=`: **`c1`**: a visible `<button id="open">` whose click calls a hidden `<input id="in" type="file">`'s `click()`, plus a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">` (for CHOOSE-4's human-style click); **`label`**: a `<label id="lab" for="in2">` and `<input id="in2" type="file" multiple>`, and the input as a target; **`none`**: a button whose click does nothing, and a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">`; **`picker`**: a button that calls `showOpenFilePicker()`; **`single`**: a button opening a single-file input, and a visible `<input id="vis" type="file">`; **`dir`**: a button opening a `webkitdirectory` input; **`dis`**: a button opening a `disabled` input; **`late49`** and **`late51`**: a button whose click arms a `setTimeout` calling the hidden input's `click()` after 4 900 ms and 5 100 ms (the page records `late.opened=<yes|no|error>` itself); **`human`**: a no-op button and a second visible `<input id="second" type="file">`; **`frame`**: a same-origin `<iframe src="s12-chooser-frame.html">` holding a button and a hidden input. Every query sets `window.__s12ch = {clicks, change, files, bytes, bytesEqual, late}` (the page counts its own `click` and `change` events, reads the received file's size and compares a page-computed digest with the one the helper passed in the URL hash `#sum=<hex>`; names and digests never leave the page). (5) `test-fixtures/s12-chooser-frame.html` (the iframe's document). (6) `test-fixtures/s12-console.html` gains the queries **`cap`** (200 messages `console.log("s12-cap-<i>:" padded with x to 2000 characters, SENTINEL included in the pad's first 40 characters)`, i = 1…200). KEEP rows use `s12-console.html?s12=con` (calls: `evaluate`, `consoleMessages`, a trusted `click` on the page's own `<body>`, a `screenshot` with `mode: "cdp"`, an `uploadFiles` on `s11-upload.html`) and `s12-chooser.html?s12=single` for KEEP-2's control; G2 rows use `e2e-playground.html?s12=g2a` (its text input `#tb`, its `click` counter read by `evaluate`) and `e2e-playground.html?s12=g2b` in a second window. The own-page, `file:` and blocked-host navigation targets of KEEP-3 are part A's (`chrome-extension://<id>/options.html`, the wave's `file:` fixture, `s12blk.localhost:8081`). No other port; each leg closes its own tab.

| R19-KEEP-1 ★ (0.11 KEEP-1; the kept session across sequential calls, answers as today) | B; JS on, armed | On `…/s12-console.html?s12=con` (a tab the wave opened) after `IDLE ≥ 20 000`: five calls, **3 000 ms apart** (inside the 5 s bound): `evaluate "1 + 1"`, `consoleMessages`, a trusted `click`, `screenshot {mode: "cdp"}`, `evaluate "(a) => a" {args: 7}`; the sampler runs from 500 ms before the first call to 4 500 ms after the last reply | `KEEPSEQ R19-KEEP-1 calls=5 gap-ms=3000 answers=5/5 banner=continuous end-ms=<e> poll-ms=<p≤250>`; answers: `2`, `entries=11`, `ok`, an image of non-zero size, `7` | **All five answers equal today's** (the value `2`, 11 entries, `ok`, a non-empty PNG, `7`: the phase doc's "answers do not change", 0.6), **the banner `continuous` from the first call to the last reply plus 4 500 ms** (a session kept inside the bound cannot end earlier than its idle bound; a `none` read inside the window means it was dropped), `end-ms` recorded for KEEP-2. A call answering differently is the STOP `existing-tool-changed`. **The banner clause is `NOT DISCRIMINATING`, and the row prints `ROW R19-KEEP-1 PASS banner-clause=NOT DISCRIMINATING`: so `PASS` stands for the answers unchanged only, never for "one attach for N calls".** A banner gap (`banner=gaps:<m>`) between calls 3 s apart does fail the row (a kept session cannot drop there); `continuous` proves nothing about keeping. **What the row cannot show, written now:** how many attaches happened (no counter exists and none is built, #351 (2)); one attach for N calls is proved by S4a/S4b's fake-counted unit tests, and this row proves their live consequence only: answers unchanged and a banner that does not drop between calls 3 s apart. A per-call build also passes the banner clause when Chrome's linger is over 3 s (Stage 0 measured ~5.1 s), so the row **fails a build that does not keep only if its linger is short**; KEEP-2's control is the discriminating row. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-1` → `Invoke-S11Sampler`. Source: 0.11 seed KEEP-1; `S4b.md` §7 (KEEP-1: one attach across sequential CDP tools, steady banner, answers as today); phase doc 0.6 |
| R19-KEEP-2 ★ (0.11 KEEP-2; idle detach and the banner's end time, with a per-call control) | B; JS on, armed | On the `s12=con` tab: (idle leg) one `evaluate "1"`, then no call, the sampler to 25 000 ms after the reply; **three times**, each after `idle ≥ 20 000`; (ctl leg) on `…/s12-chooser.html?s12=single` three times: `tab.upload(<single-input button>, [<ROOT>\s11-1k.bin, <ROOT>\s11-sent.bin], {chooser: true})`, which refuses after the interception is on (the single input takes one file) and so **ends the use with an error and drops the session at once** (spec §10 S5a note: "Any refusal or error after the interception was asked for also ends the use with an error, so the keeper detaches the session (#347)"); the sampler to 25 000 ms after the reply, each after `idle ≥ 20 000` | `KEEPEND R19-KEEP-2 leg=idle since-last-call-ms=0 end-ms=<e1> next=ok` ×3 and `KEEPEND R19-KEEP-2 leg=ctl since-last-call-ms=0 end-ms=<c1> next=BAD_PARAMS` ×3 (the ctl refusal is `SINGLE_FILE_INPUT_MESSAGE`: `the file input takes one file, and more than one path was given`, `extension/src/content/upload-target.ts:31`) | **Median(idle `end-ms`) − median(ctl `end-ms`) ≥ 3 500 ms** (the idle bound is 5 000 ms, phase doc 0.6; 1 500 ms of margin), **and the spread of the three ctl readings ≤ 3 000 ms**, else the clause is NOT MEASURED (the control has no resolution: the old linger was 3–14 s, SAFE-4 (b)); every `end-ms` ≤ 25 000 (the banner ends). The three values and both medians are recorded (V3-4 and V4-1 of `S4b.md` §5 wait on them). **Discrimination:** a per-call build detaches at the reply on both legs, so idle − ctl ≈ 0 and fails; a build that never detaches shows no `none` by 25 000 and fails; the ctl leg is the same CDP path with an immediate detach, so the same linger enters both medians. The row writes down what S4b did not: the sampler measures the banner, not the session, so a banner-side change by Chrome would move both legs alike and cancel. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-2`. Source: 0.11 seed KEEP-2; `S4b.md` §7 (KEEP-2); phase doc 0.6; spec §10 S5a note (the refusal that drops the session) |
| R19-KEEP-3 ★ (0.11 KEEP-3; a disarm and a page that turns blocked, own or `file:` end the session; the gate re-check) | B; **disarmed** then on; block list gains `s12blk.localhost` then restored | Each leg on a tab the wave opened, after `idle ≥ 20 000`: a CDP call (`evaluate "1"`) then **1 000 ms later** the event, then one more call 1 000 ms after it: (disarm) the toolbar button pressed (off) then on after the call; (block) the pattern `s12blk.localhost` added in the wave's options window for a tab on that host loaded before it, then removed; (nav-blocked) the tab navigated to `s12blk.localhost:8081` with the pattern set; (nav-own) the tab navigated to `chrome-extension://<id>/options.html`; (nav-file) navigated to the wave's `file:` fixture (only with `TOGGLE reading on`, else `NOT RUN — waits for G11-3`); (close) the tab closed by `tab.close()`; (replace) **not triggered** (no deterministic way to cause a Memory-Saver replace is sourced): `NOT RUN`. That no attach follows a disarm or a refused page is proved live by the answers (`kept-session-used`) and by the unit tests (the gate at receipt and again before the attach); `attach-after-disarm` and `attach-on-refused-page` are **NOT DISCRIMINATING in this row** (Open item 5). **Discrimination:** a build that keeps the session across the disarm ends at the idle bound, so `end-ms` equals the idle median (fails the 2 500 ms margin) and a build that skips the re-check answers `ok` (STOP). | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-keep-3` (`Set-S9BlockList`, the toolbar press). | R19-G2-2 ★ (G6.8; a typed string complete under a competing capture) | C; as R19-G2-1 | A: raw `client.call("type", {tab, ref, text: <40-character fixture string>, trusted: true})` into `#tb`; B loops default screenshots of its tab (at least 3, the first within 50 ms of A's send); **five rounds**, the input cleared between rounds | `G2TYPE round=<i> sent=40 got=40 lost=0 b-calls=<c≥3> b-ok=<c> b-lock-timeout=0` ×5 | A's `type` answers `ok` and the input value (read by `evaluate`, counted) equals the string in **5 of 5 rounds**, every B screenshot `ok`, none `LOCK_TIMEOUT` under a 30 s timeout. **The clause S6-G2 said cannot be observed live, quoted:** "No B screenshot is forwarded between A's daemon `focusTab` and A's input reply" (`S6-G2-builder.md` §7, line 128; the verifier's MINOR-4: "No log line or status field shows when the daemon forwards. A live runner sees only client-side send and reply times", `S6-G2-verifier.md`). **How the row stands without it, in client-observable terms (the verifier's fix):** (i) no B screenshot sent after A's send replies before A's reply + 450 ms (`gap-ms` of every B call is printed; a B call already holding the lock before A's send is not in the set); (ii) `daemon.status.lock.holder` reads A during the hold (R19-G2-1's reading); (iii) the effect, A's text complete. The row **cannot prove** that no capture came between the focus and the input in a round where the text arrived intact; it fails a build that lets one in only when the capture steals focus and a character is lost (`lost>0`), and five rounds raise that chance. Written plainly: a pass is evidence of no harm, not of the absence of an interleaving. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-g2-2`. Source: `S6-G2.md` §7 and `S6-G2-builder.md` §7 (G6.8 drafts); `S6-G2-verifier.md` MINOR-4; decision #354 |

After the row: the current build rebuilt, reloaded and **R19-0 re-run with `LOADED … args=answered`, else STOP `old-build-left-loaded`**. | R19-LOGS ★ (R7.5, per slice; a NEW row, never an edit of R18-LOGS; #349) | A, B, C, D, E (end of each, after R19-RESTORE) | Over **this slice's own** console capture (`console-<slice>.txt` for a slice with no extension reload; **`console-<slice>-<k>.txt` segments for B, D and E, see Capture segments**; the control line `safety: disarmed by the user` written by R19-RESTORE's press, in the last segment), `daemon.log` since the slice's mark, and each actor's tool **results** (never its inputs), as §4.26's R18-LOGS; **the actors scan reads product text only: the MCP failure report's screenshot line and the client's image line are counted separately and named** (#349); the console/daemon scans also carry what part A deferred here: **S3a check 9** ("A sentinel in the console text appears in the reply only. It is absent from the worker console and `daemon.log`") and **S3b's CON-LOGS** ("a sentinel logged by the fixture is counted 0 in `daemon.log` and the worker console"), and S2b's `args` sentinel (**the args sentinel** is the fixture constant of R19-ARGS-1's fifth table value, a value distinct from `SENTINEL`, defined by S7-helpers and passed to the scanners by argument as `SENTINEL` is) | Per slice: `SCAN console: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other 0) token=0 sentinel=0 profile=0`; `SCAN daemon: … sentinel=0 …`; `DAEMONLOG <slice> sentinel=0 console-text=0 args-text=0`; `SCAN actors: ://=<n> (fixture <n>, other 0) token=0 sentinel=<n> profile-other=0 shot-line=<a> image-line=<b>` with `profile-all=<a+b+…>` and one `SCANTOOL` per tool; `CONSOLE <slice> segments=<k> capture=<file(s)> texts=<n> control-disarmed=<n≥1 in the last segment>` | **Token, sentinel (fixture constants `SENTINEL`, the args sentinel, passed by argument), profile path and every non-fixture `scheme://host` are 0 in the console and daemon streams** (a non-zero count is a STOP, `text-leak`); `DAEMONLOG` probes 0 (`SENTINEL`, `s12-con-`, `s12-notea`, the args sentinel). **In the actors' results the sentinel is expected, by design, only in the tools whose reply carries it** (`browser_console_messages`, and `browser_evaluate`'s own echoed value): `SCANTOOL` shows `sentinel>0` for those tools only and **0 for every other tool** (a refusal line, a snapshot, an upload answer); `token=0` everywhere; **`profile-other=0`** (matches outside the two named lines); `shot-line` and `image-line` are printed as the two findings of #349 (a) and (b), expected only in a failure-report-bearing result (`shot-line`) and an image item (`image-line`), **never counted against the row** but each must match its own form, else it is counted in `profile-other`. The capture holds the control line (`control-disarmed` ≥ 1, in the last segment), else NOT MEASURED for that slice; a segment not saved before a reload (`CAPSEG … saved=no`) also makes it NOT MEASURED; a token that cannot be found is NOT MEASURED. **Discrimination:** Run 18's slice A read `profile=2` from exactly these two lines and could not tell a product leak from them; a leak in a refusal text, a snapshot or an upload answer lands in `profile-other` and fails the row, and a sentinel in `daemon.log` or the worker console (a logging regression) is a STOP. | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-logs` → `S9 scan-log`, `S9 scan-file`, `S11 scan`, the actors scanner split by tool. Source: decision #349 (the new-row rule); §4.26 R18-LOGS; `S3a-builder.md` §7 check 9, `S3b-builder.md` §7 CON-LOGS; phase doc 0.10 (log invariant) |
| R19-RESTORE ★ (R7.4, per slice) | A, B, C, D, E (end of each) | As §4.26 `R18-RESTORE` (one toolbar press pair, off, at least 1.5 s, on; the options page opened fresh; armed, JavaScript, **block list** and Upload folders set to the found values only where they differ; found-text files deleted after a match; stored values, `daemon.status`, the toolbar title and `RULES` read back), **plus**: the block list read back **by count and hash** (R19 edits it), every native file dialog the slice caused closed and counted by UIA, window X and every DevTools window closed (the worker-console capture window last, after the control press and the last segment's save: Capture segments), the wave's options windows closed, and after slices D and E the **current build** loaded (R19-0's `LOADED` line equal to the slice's first) | `armed`, `evaluate`, block list and Upload folders equal to R19-0 of the same slice **by count and sha256 (counts shown, hashes not printed**, except the empty text's `e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855`); `RULES 0`; `DIALOGS <slice> native=0 devtools=0 wave-windows-open=0`; `RESTORED: …` | As R18-RESTORE: all five state items equal to R19-0 of the same slice (R7.4); `DIALOGS` all 0; any mismatch, a dialog or a DevTools window still open, or a slice D/E that ends on the old build is a STOP (`not-restored`, `dialog-left-open`, `devtools-left-open`, `old-build-left-loaded`) that escalates (integrity floor). | S7-helpers: `R19 r19-restore` → `Restore-S11Safety`, `S11 restore-check`, `B17 rules`, the UIA dialog count. Source: §4.26 R18-RESTORE; phase doc R7.4; STOP rules |

**Slices and order (#271).** One server (8081) and the Prologue as §4.26 (ledger: preflight, server, files `<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>`, `s11-1k.bin`, `s11-sent.bin`, `s11-out.bin`, the sentinel folder, a `file:` fixture outside the repo, **the slice's own console capture started before R19-0**, `npm run build` from a clean tree at a committed HEAD, reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1`). **R19-0 is the first row of each slice and proves the loaded build before any state change (R7.2);** **R19-RESTORE then R19-LOGS end every slice**; a row that changes the safety state restores it before the next row. Runner attempts: at most three per slice (phase doc § S7 as S1; §7's fix rounds apply).
- **liveA19 (args and console; JS on, armed; the block list and JS switch are edited here):** R19-0; **R19-BANNER-CTL**; R19-ARGS-1, R19-ARGS-2, R19-ARGS-GLOBAL, R19-ARGS-CSP, R19-ARGS-STOP; R19-CON-1, R19-CON-2, R19-CON-REJ, R19-CON-BIG, R19-CON-NAV, R19-CON-MCP, R19-CON-CAP, then, each restored before the next: **R19-CON-BANNER** (its own negative leg first, 20 s idle), R19-ARGS-3 (JS off), R19-CON-JSOFF (JS off), R19-CON-3, R19-CON-FRAME, R19-CON-NOTEA (block list gains `s12blk.localhost`), R19-CON-4 and R19-CON-FLOOR (toolbar disarm), R19-CON-OWN, R19-CON-FILE (only with `TOGGLE reading on`); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS. No DevTools window opens in this slice other than the worker-console capture window, the wave's one named exception (Capture segments).
- **liveB19 (the kept session, the carried banner rows, DevTools; JS on, armed; ROOT added):** R19-0; **the row-less `DEVPROBE` (window X opened, DevTools opened by the helper's method, `evaluate` until K-devt or 15 s, X closed; its line printed; an `attached=no` result makes R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4 and R19-SWEEP-HELD `NOT MEASURED — DevTools was not attached`)**; **the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER) right after it, before any row: R19-KEEP-2's ctl leg is the slice's one `chooser: true` call and can raise a native dialog if the interception fails**; **R19-UP-OPT first among the rows, after the investigator's report** (the investigator is a read-only leaf run before this slice, no state change; #351 (1)); R19-BANNER-CTL; R19-UP-6; R19-F-RAW, -2, -DIR; R19-KEEP-1; R19-KEEP-2; R19-KEEP-3 (disarm, block list; each restored before the next); R19-CON-KEPT; R19-KEEP-OVERLAP leg a; **then the DevTools rows, after every non-DevTools row of the slice:** R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4, R19-DEVT-NOFLAG, and **last R19-SWEEP-HELD** (it reloads the extension; the capture is saved before the reload and reopened after it, before the re-run, Capture segments; R19-0 is re-run after it as a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS.
- **liveC19 (the file chooser and G2; JS on, armed; ROOT added; window X in front for CHOOSE-4):** R19-0; **R19-BANNER-CTL; the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER), before every row that can raise a native dialog**; **R19-CHOOSE-1, R19-CHOOSE-LABEL, R19-CHOOSE-REFUSE first** (S5a V6); R19-CHOOSE-2, R19-CHOOSE-3, R19-CHOOSE-MCP (legs a–c, e), R19-CHOOSE-KEPT, R19-CHOOSE-FRAME, R19-CHOOSE-HUMAN, R19-CHOOSE-LATE, R19-KEEP-OVERLAP leg b; **R19-CHOOSE-4** (three native dialogs, each closed before the next leg); R19-CHOOSE-DISARM (toolbar), R19-CHOOSE-JSOFF and R19-CHOOSE-MCP leg d (JS off, restored); R19-G2-1, R19-G2-2 (two clients); R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS (the dialogs' closure read by `DIALOGS`).
- **liveD19 (old build, args):** **R19-0 first, in full, on the current build** (its `LOADED … args=answered` and R18-0's state lines are the slice's reference for R19-RESTORE, R7.4); the swap to the pre-S2a build (capture segment saved before, reopened after); the old-build proof (`LOADED consoleMessages=UNKNOWN_METHOD …`, a line of its own, not R19-0); **R19-ARGS-OLD** (part A: the wave's last row in its slice); the current build reloaded (segment saved before, reopened after, before the re-run), **R19-0 re-run (a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only)**; R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS. **liveE19 (old build, chooser):** **R19-0 first, in full, on the current build**; `63c771c` built and loaded (segments as in liveD19), **R19-VERSION**, the current build reloaded, **R19-0 re-run (a `LOADED rerun=<k> …` line only)**; R19-RESTORE; R19-LOGS.
- **Where the carried and the control rows sit:** each of liveA19, liveB19 and liveC19 holds one R19-BANNER-CTL at 20 000 ms idle (the Banner rule); R19-CON-BANNER's own negative and after legs, in liveA19, are 20 000 ms too (Open item 8); the **UP-OPT investigator is not a row** and runs once before liveB19; **the DevTools probe is the only DevTools step before liveB19's DevTools rows**. No G1 row (G1 waits for G12-2, #337).
- **STOP lines matched to a return (R7.6):** every `STOP <reason>` line (31) is followed by a `RETURN <seq> after-stop=<reason> restored=<yes|no|n/a> readback=<equal|n/a>` before the next row; the reasons and where they arise: `attach-after-disarm` (R19-CON-4 only, under the Banner rule; R19-KEEP-3 and R19-CHOOSE-DISARM carry none); `attach-on-refused-page` (R19-CON-3, -OWN, -FILE, -JSOFF, R19-ARGS-3, R19-CHOOSE-2, -JSOFF, under the Banner rule; not R19-KEEP-3); `kept-session-used` (R19-KEEP-3: an `ok` where the gate refuses); `dialog-not-opened` (R19-CHOOSE-4, -DISARM: no dialog on the human-style click; it is the one reason for "the interception is still on", and no other row prints a separate reason for it); `outside-file-reached-page` (R19-CHOOSE-2, -HUMAN, any upload row); `args-ran-js-off` (R19-ARGS-3); `existing-tool-changed` (R19-ARGS-1's controls, R19-CON-MCP, R19-CON-KEPT, R19-KEEP-1, R19-CHOOSE-JSOFF, R19-CHOOSE-MCP leg e); `dialog-left-open` (R19-CLOSER, R19-CHOOSE-*, R19-RESTORE) and `devtools-left-open` (R19-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4, R19-SWEEP-HELD, R19-RESTORE); `not-restored` (R19-RESTORE); `text-leak` (R19-LOGS); `old-build-left-loaded` (R19-ARGS-OLD, R19-VERSION, R19-RESTORE); `throw-after-state` (any slice). R7.6 passes when every `STOP` has exactly one `RETURN` after it, in order, and the final ledger of each slice holds no unmatched line either way.

**Capture segments (R7.5 across reloads; fix round 1).** The slice's console capture is a DevTools window on the extension's service worker (`recipes/s11-r16.ps1`, block `console`: `CONSOLE the worker console window is open`), and an extension reload replaces that worker, so the window and what it showed are lost (Run 17b lost a capture that way, #318). Slices B (R19-SWEEP-HELD's reload), D and E (two reloads each) therefore keep the capture in **segments**: before every reload the helper saves the capture to the slice's next file (`console-<slice>-<k>.txt`, k = 1, 2, …; the paths all differ and a path that already holds text is a STOP, as §4.26's guard), prints `CAPSEG <slice> seg=<k> saved=<yes|no> before=<reload|end>` and closes the capture window; after the reload and **before** R19-0's re-run it reopens a worker console (the helper of the `console` block), so the re-run's worker output is captured, as the Prologue starts the capture before R19-0. R19-LOGS scans **all segments of the slice as one stream** (counts summed), and the control line (R19-RESTORE's press) must be in the **last** segment. A segment not saved before a reload (`saved=no`) makes R19-LOGS NOT MEASURED for the slice. Slices A and C reload nothing and keep one segment (`console-liveA19.txt`, `console-liveC19.txt`). **The capture window is the wave's one named exception to every "no DevTools" sentence** (as §4.26's capture): it is not a window X, `DIALOGS … devtools=<n>` counts DevTools windows other than it, and R19-RESTORE saves the last segment and closes it before that read, so `devtools=0` holds with no capture window left; a capture window still open at the slice's end is the STOP `devtools-left-open`, as any DevTools window.

**Open items, recorded before the run (not failures; the session agent rules, never a builder):** From part A: (1) **R19-ARGS-OLD needs a pre-S2a build** (`3ab9207`, a scratch worktree with its own `npm ci`, never a shared `node_modules`); `NOT RUN` if it cannot be loaded. (2) **Bounds part A chose without a source:** `STOPCALL ms` 1 500–3 500; ±500 ms around the toolbar press in R19-CON-FLOOR; the page's 8/1/1 level mix. (3) **Class of `consoleMessages`:** phase doc 0.5 says class `tab`; #334 and the spec's S3a note class it `evaluate` (JavaScript off refuses it); the sheet follows #334. (4) **Whether Chrome put a frame out of process is not observable by an SDK actor** (`Target.getTargets` is refused through `chrome.debugger`, Stage 0 C5); R19-CON-FRAME builds the two cases by the site rule (a same-site pair on `*.s12site.localhost`, a cross-site pair `localhost` / `127.0.0.1`) and its two legs prove it together; `a-nopattern entries=2` makes leg a's drop clause NOT DISCRIMINATING. (5) **No attach counter exists and none is built (#351 (2))**; fix round 1 struck every "counted by the keeper" clause and put the **Banner rule** in its place (20 s idle plus a per-slice control). **Not discriminating live, by design:** `attach-after-disarm` and `attach-on-refused-page` in R19-KEEP-3, `attach-after-disarm` in R19-CHOOSE-DISARM, and R19-KEEP-1's banner clause; their proof is the answers (`kept-session-used`) and the unit tests (the gate at receipt and again before the attach). Whether that gap is acceptable or the instrument (G12-2) is wanted is the session agent's to rule; the step agent raises it, unconditionally, under agent-rules §3 item 9 (a check that cannot be made discriminating) and reports it upward. From part B: (6) *Closed by fix round 1:* S5c's texts are committed (`11a1210`, `d38b590`) and quoted into R19-VERSION. (7) **The pre-S5a build is `63c771c`** (S4b, the last commit before S5a's spec note `8eab22c`; named in decision #352); S5c's own commit does not matter to it. (10) **The sampler measures the banner, not the session**, so R19-KEEP-1 cannot count attaches and R19-KEEP-2 is a differential against a control; if KEEP-2's control has no resolution (spread over 3 000 ms), KEEP-2 and KEEP-3's timing clauses are NOT MEASURED and the kept session is proved only by its tests. (11) **The DevTools opening method is unproven** (F12 attached nothing twice; no sourced method); the `DEVPROBE` decides, and every DevTools row is NOT MEASURED without it. **The infobar's Cancel button by UIA** has no sourced method (R19-KEEP-4's cancel leg is optional). (12) **Closing Chrome's native file dialog is unproven** (#251): S7-helpers' closer (owner window X, close pattern, Cancel, Escape to that window only) needs its own unit tests, and **the closer is exercised first by the row-less `CLOSERPROBE` (R19-CLOSER) on a dialog the helper opens itself, under a 5 000 ms watchdog, before the first chooser call of liveB19 (R19-KEEP-2's ctl leg) and of liveC19 (R19-CHOOSE-1 onward can raise a dialog if the interception fails); a failing closer is the returning STOP `dialog-left-open`.** (13) **Memory-Saver replace** (R19-KEEP-3's `replace` leg) has no sourced trigger: `NOT RUN`. ### 4.28 R20 rows (Step 13 S5-sheet, Run 20: the Step 13 feature rows and Run 19's re-measured rows)

*Written 2026-10-29 by Step 13 slice S5-sheet (Claude Sonnet 5.5, medium), before any Run 20 row ran, before the S5 helpers were built and before a Run 20 build was loaded (exit check R5.1). No observation was made to write it: no Chrome, no daemon, no page. Sources: phase doc `docs/plans/2026-10-29-step13-phase-f-followups.md` § 0.8 as amended by decisions.md #391 (its seven changes win), the Dated note, § S5, § Exit checks R5.1-R5.6, § Human gates, § STOP rules and forbidden fixes, § As built (Stage 0, S1, S2, S3b, S4); decisions #382, #384-#398; `…-followups-steps/Stage0.md` § Measured facts and §5, `stage0-m2-D.txt` (the DevTools controls); `docs/plans/2026-10-27-step12-phase-f-steps/S7-run-record.md` §4-§6; the Step 12 phase doc's Follow-ups bullet citing #373; §4.27 (the form and the rows re-measured) and the Run 19 record in §5; `recipes/CLAUDE.md`. §4.23-§4.27 and the Run 16-19 records are not edited. The ids are new (`R20-…`); a re-measurement names its R19 row and is a new row, never an edit (agent-rules §2, #382). S3a was not built (#384, #387): `R20-CON-NOTEA` is the documented limit, and no S3a-only row exists.*

**Pass rules, written before any observation.** §4.27's preamble applies unchanged: a row passes when every clause of its pass rule holds and each quoted text matches character for character; `RECORD` passes by being recorded; `NOT MEASURED` and `NOT RUN` never count as a pass; **an `ok` is never evidence** (the evidence is a code and message, a count from the page's own reporter or effect counter, a count of `daemon.log` or the worker console, a control read with its own source); a failed row is recorded and the wave goes on; nothing is re-run, re-tuned or fixed in the wave; an expected text is never edited after its run. Nothing the sheet lists as never printed is printed: no sentinel, console text, `args` value, path, URL, title, user name or token (counts only; the record may quote a fixture's constant label such as `s12=con`). The sentinel is the fixture constant `SENTINEL` of `s12-console.js`, passed to the scanners by argument. **Each re-measured row says which R19 row it re-measures and what changed in the reading; its pass rule is a new one, not the old one edited.** A row that fails with a passing control is a product finding with its fix a proposed decision, not a STOP, unless a STOP line below names it (phase doc § STOP rules, "Continues anyway"). **No R20 row judges the "extension is debugging this browser" banner:** Run 19 judged it with a control in each slice and R20 re-measures none of those clauses, so §4.27's Banner rule, `R19-BANNER-CTL` and the banner STOPs are not carried; the effect counters and the answers decide.

**Readers of MCP legs (Step 12 S7-run-record §4 item 8; `mcp/CLAUDE.md`).** A reader of an MCP leg matches the MCP's own line and `isError`, never the extension's message and never a code on line 1. The three safety lines are the SDK's (`safetyRefusalLine`, `sdk/src/error.ts`), printed with `isError: true`: `DISARMED: the user has turned the bridge off in Chrome. Stop and ask the user to turn it back on; do not retry.` (`DISARMED_ALL_LINE`); `JS_DISABLED: the user has turned JavaScript off for the bridge in Chrome, so evaluate (browser_evaluate) is refused; every other call still works. Ask the user before relying on it; do not retry it.` (`JS_DISABLED_LINE`); `DOMAIN_BLOCKED` is not used by a Run 20 MCP leg. An error that is neither a safety nor an attach error (`VERSION`, the chooser-busy refusal) reaches the agent as the **bare message**, one text item with `isError: true` (`mcp/test/s13-chooser-busy.test.ts` asserts `content` equal to `[{type: "text", text: <message>}]`). A reader that finds the extension's text, or expects a code on line 1, on an MCP leg is the instrument at fault: the row is NOT DISCRIMINATING, never a pass. The SDK and raw legs read the extension's own code, message and `data`.

**STOP rule (#193), §4.27's and the phase doc's added.** §4.27's STOP rule is carried whole except its banner STOPs (above): a failed restore; a guard printing STOP; a human gate such as an unpinned toolbar button; a port held by a program the wave did not start; an unreadable file-URL toggle; a found state that is not armed with JavaScript on; a tracked file changed outside the steps dir; any throw after the first state change; a native dialog left open; a DevTools window left open (the worker-console capture window is the one named exception until its slice's R20-RESTORE); a `CLAUDE.md` runtime invariant that would have to change; a safety state not restored; a file reaching a page from outside the Upload folders; console text, an `args` value, a path, a pattern, a URL, the user's name or the token in a log or in committed evidence; a port-9234 holder that is not the daemon, or an orphan process; anything pushed or tagged; a pre-registered check that fails after the allowed fix rounds. On any STOP line the runner restores first if the safety state was touched, records the read-back by count and hash, writes the ledger and returns. **Added by the Step 13 phase doc (§ STOP rules), those that bind Run 20:** a **popup leased to a client that does not hold its source tab** (`popup-lease-wrong`: R20-POP-BG, -FG, -OTHER, -NONE); a **navigation URL kept past the join or logged** (`text-leak`, found by R20-LOGS); the **extension disabled or asking for approval after the reload** (`extension-disabled`: R20-0, -0B, -0C read it); an **existing tool answering differently on a call that does not touch the changed paths** (`existing-tool-changed`: the controls named in each row; key order aside); a **native dialog or DevTools window left open** (`dialog-left-open`, `devtools-left-open`); **file-chooser interception still on after its call ended** (`chooser-interception-left-on`: the control upload in R20-CHOOSE-TWICE and R20-CHOOSE-KEPT must be `ok`); an **`evaluate` with `args` running while JavaScript is off** (`args-ran-js-off`: R20-ARGS-3's effect counter above its control); the **loaded build not the HEAD build** (`build-not-loaded`: R20-0, -0B, -0C); and an **old build left loaded** (`old-build-left-loaded`: R20-ARGS-OLD, R20-VERSION, R20-RESTORE). **Forbidden fixes** (phase doc): a permission beyond `webNavigation`; a router method added for G1 without both tables; storing or logging navigation URLs; reading a console object through page code; sorting or re-ordering keys anywhere to make a row pass; a changed message on any path other than the second-chooser refusal; a new code without the §10 note first; editing an R18 or R19 row, or a row's expected text after its run; a reader that matches the extension's text on an MCP leg; and every forbidden fix of Steps 9-12 still binding. A helper may be fixed only between rows, never past a STOP line. Every STOP or guard line printed is matched to a return in the ledger (R5.6).

**Human gates and windows.** As §4.27's, with the Step 13 phase doc's § Human gates: only windows the wave opened are touched (the human's tab is the wave's own `W` tab); safety state changes go through the options page in the wave's own window or the toolbar button (pinned; an unpinned button is a STOP, never a mouse click); the block list and Upload folders are edited only in the wave's own options window and restored exactly; **the file-URL toggle is read, never pressed**; DevTools is opened only in a window the wave opened on a fixture page (undocked included) and closed by the wave; no Chrome restart, the reload is by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1` (#65, one re-run on exit 2, #99); a native dialog the wave causes is closed by the wave. **G13-3: fixture pages only, on `127.0.0.1`, `localhost` and `*.localhost`, never a real site or credentials; no profile path and no user name in evidence.** The manifest's `webNavigation` permission is not a G13-2 matter (#393); the reload prompts nothing, and R20-0 reads that the extension is enabled. A push or tag is G13-1 and not done.

**Fixtures and ports.** No port is hard-coded as free. The preflight of each slice runs `netstat -ano` and `Get-NetTCPConnection` (a **Bound** entry with no listener counts as held, as Run 19's 8082), picks `<P>` as the lowest port from 8081 up that appears in neither listing, and prints the ledger line `PORT <P> checked=<UTC time> listed=<n>`; the choice is re-checked just before `python -m http.server <P> --bind 127.0.0.1 --directory test-fixtures` starts (output to a log outside the repo), and 9234 must be free or held by the daemon (else STOP). A URL counts as **fixture** only if `http://` on `127.0.0.1:<P>`, `localhost:<P>` or `*.localhost:<P>`, `chrome://extensions`, or this extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>/` pages. **Existing pages used** (unchanged): `test-fixtures/e2e-playground.html` (its `s12=` queries `a1`, `a3`, `bc`, `g2a`, `g2b`; `?popup=1`, the page the playground's "Open popup" link opens; the `#open-popup` link, `target=_blank`), `test-fixtures/s12-console.html` + `s12-console.js` (`con`: 10 messages and 1 uncaught error; `tick`; `cap`: 200 messages of 2 000 characters; `notea`: an `Error` object from `s12blk.localhost`'s `s12-notea.js`; `noteaok`: the same from `localhost`'s `s12-noteaok.js`; **both script URLs hard-code port 8081** (`test-fixtures/s12-console.js`, the `script(...)` calls), so R20-CON-NOTEA needs `<P>` = 8081: if the preflight finds 8081 held, both its legs are NOT MEASURED "fixture port" and no port-relative copy is made), `test-fixtures/s12-chooser.html` + `s12-chooser.js` (`c1`, `none`: a button that opens nothing, `single`), `test-fixtures/s11-upload.html` (the plain upload control). **New page, S5-helpers (described here, none built by this sheet): `s13-chooser.html` + `s13-chooser.js`, to be added under the `test-fixtures` directory**, query `s13=`: **`kept`**: a visible button whose click opens a hidden `<input id="in" type="file">`, and the page's own reporter `window.__s13ch = {clicks, input, change, cancel, files, size, digest}` (it counts its own `click`, `input`, `change` and `cancel` events on the input, reads the received file's size and a page-computed SHA-256 digest; names and digests never leave the page, only equal-or-not does); **`disflip`**: a button whose click handler runs `in.click(); in.disabled = true;` in one task (the input is enabled at the click and disabled when the extension reads it); **`disself`**: a visible `<input id="dis" type="file" disabled>` and a counter; **`disbtn`**: a button that clicks an input that is disabled from the start (Run 19's `dis` shape, the control of the reachability statement). Each page sets `window.__s13ch` before its first paint; the driver window guard learns its title (#367). Every other row uses an existing page.

**Line forms (Run 20).** Existing, unchanged and carried from §4.26 and §4.27 by reference: `ROW <id> <STOP|FAIL|NOT DISCRIMINATING|NOT MEASURED|NOT RUN|PASS|RECORD>[ …]` (**exactly one `ROW` per id and slice**), `CHECK`, `ANSWER <row> <code or ok> …`, `CALL`, `STATE`, `TOGGLE`, `FOUNDLIST`, `RULES <n>`, `HEAD <12 hex> tree=<clean or dirty>`, the first line `MANIFEST …` of §4.26's R18-0, `CON`, `CONREF`, `CONFLOOR`, `CHOOSE`, `G2`, `VERSIONCH`, `OLD`, `EFF`, `ARGS` (both its per-answer and its `total` form), `KEEPDT`, `SWEEPHELD`, `RESTORED: …`, `DIALOGS`, `STOP`/`RETURN`, `SCAN`, `CAPSEG`. **Redefined (S5-helpers builds the new meaning, the old reader must not be reused):** `CONCAP lines=<k+1> body-chars=<c> marker=yes first-index=<200-k+1> last-index=200 sentinel=<s>` (§4.27 (25) printed `lines=<k>`; here `lines` counts the marker line, and the marker is the #398 text); `CHOOSE` gains the judged key `names-devtools=<yes or no>` (R20-CHOOSE-TWICE: the busy text names DevTools, expected `no`), which §4.27 (21) lacks. **New** (the contract to S5-helpers; each form is compared with this section by key; every value is a count or a fixed word, never a text): (1) `LOADED20 [rerun=<k> ]keys=<page or sorted> nested=<page or sorted> elem=<page or sorted> perm=<yes or no or unread> listener=<yes or no or unread> enabled=<yes or no> args=<answered or CODE>`; (2) `KEYORDER <row> leg=<label> keys=<page or sorted> nested=<page or sorted> elem=<page or sorted> json-equal=<yes or no or n/a> via=<sdk or mcp or raw>`; (3) `POP <row> leg=<label> n=<popups> granted-to=<A or B or C or none> in-ms=<max over n> others=<grants to any other client> held-call=<TAB_HELD or ok or n/a> active-guard=<bg or fg> console-new=<n>` (`console-new` = lines the worker console printed in the capture between the first popup's open and the last grant, expected 0, POP-JOIN-1; `active-guard` = the source tab's `listTabs` active flag read just before the call); (4) `MCPLINE <row> leg=<label> is-error=<yes or no> text=<exact or contains or other> lines=<n>` (`exact` = the whole text equals the quoted line; `other` is a failed clause, never a reader's choice of another text); (5) `CHKEPT <row> leg=<different or same> call=<1 or 2> answer=<ok or CODE> clicks=<n> input=<n> change=<n> cancel=<n> files=<0 or 1> digest-differs=<yes or no or n/a>` (page counters, cumulative on the loaded page); (6) `RECT <row> leg=<label> win=<A or B or X or O> x=<n> y=<n> w=<n> h=<n> z=<index> covered-by-others-px=<n>` (window rectangles from the OS window list, numbers only, no title); (7) `CTRL20 <row> leg=<label> when=<before-open or after-open> windows=<n or n/a> title=<ok or none or n/a> attached=<yes or no or unread> targets=<n>` (`n/a` only for a docked leg, which has no window of its own) (the bridge-independent DevTools control, below); (8) `DEVT20 <row> leg=<label> answer=<ok or CODE> value=<ok or n/a> exact=<yes or no or n/a> devtools-windows-after=<n>`; (9) `FLAG debugging-flag=<yes or no or unread>` (RECORD); (10) `PORT <P> checked=<UTC time> listed=<n>`; (11) `SCAN20 <stream>: name=<n> sentinel=<n> token=<n>`; the actors' stream prints **one line per tool**, `SCAN20 actors <tool>: name=<n> sentinel=<n> token=<n>` (like §4.27's `SCANTOOL`); (12) `S4 commit=<ancestor or not>`; (13) `DOCK <R20-0C or R20-RESTORE> side=<bottom or right or left or undock or unread>` (the user's DevTools dock-side preference, read in the wave's own DevTools window, below).

*Dated note (2026-10-29, before any Run 20 observation; session agent, from `S5-helpers.md`):* form (3) also allows `n/a` for `in-ms`, `held-call` and `active-guard` on a leg with no grant to time and no source tab to read (R20-POP-NONE prints all three as `n/a`). No expected text or pass rule changes.

*Dated note (2026-10-29, before any Run 20 observation; session agent, decision #401):* R20-ARGS-ORDER's `ref` leg is not reachable: `evaluateRef` and the extension refuse a ref to a non-element value, so a keyed object has none. The driver still runs the step and records its answer (`ANSWER … ref <code>`), with no `KEYORDER … leg=ref` line; the leg is RECORD and does not count toward the row's verdict. The other legs and their pass rules are unchanged.

*Dated note (2026-10-29, before any Run 20 observation; session agent, decision #403):* (a) R20-CON-DESC, which had no line form, prints `CHECK R20-CON-DESC sentence-1=<n> sentence-2=<n> control-tool=<read or missing>`; a throw from the tool listing prints `LEG R20-CON-DESC actor-error FAIL`. (b) Slice A defers no row to R20-LOGS: no ARGS or CON row has a worker-console clause, so each prints its own `ROW` inline. (c) A POP grant earlier than the click minus 20 ms is not counted: the leg prints `CHECK <row> <leg> grant-before-click=yes` and reads NOT DISCRIMINATING. No expected text or pass rule changes.

**The bridge-independent DevTools control (#391 (1)).** Run 19's `DEVPROBE` read `attached` from the refusal code itself (`recipes/s12-forms-b.ts`), which explains its `attached=no` when the bridge simply was not refused. For Run 20 the control is read **before** the bridge's call, from two sources the call does not touch, both measured in Stage 0 (`stage0-m2-D.txt`, Chrome 156, scratch profile with the debugging flag): (a) **the window list of the wave's Chrome process**: an undocked DevTools is a top-level window titled `DevTools - <fixture host>:<P>/<path>?<query>` (11 of 11 undocked legs; count of such windows 0 before the open, 1 after; the title text is compared and never printed); a docked pane has no window of its own, so (a) reads 0 and is not judged for a docked leg; (b) **`chrome.debugger.getTargets()` evaluated in the extension's service-worker console** (the wave's worker-console capture window, opened before the slice's R20-0): the fixture tab's entry reads `attached: false` before the open and `attached: true` after it (Stage 0: a tab held only by DevTools reads `attached: true`; the page target `attached` went false to true in 11 of 11 legs). The control passes when `after-open` reads `attached=yes` (and, for an undocked leg, `windows=1 title=ok`) and `before-open` read `attached=no`. A control that cannot be read (the worker console cannot evaluate, or DevTools cannot be opened or undocked by the helper's method) makes the row **RECORD** (`devtools-control-unreadable`), as #391's Revisit-if says, never a PASS; a control that reads `attached=no` after the open makes the row NOT MEASURED "DevTools was not attached". The flag that Stage 0's scratch Chrome ran with (`--enable-unsafe-extension-debugging`) is not known for the user's Chrome; `FLAG` records it by the process list (yes or no, no command line printed), and the flagless case stays a RECORD (Run 19's `R19-DEVT-NOFLAG`; #383).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **liveA20** | JS on, armed; the block list and JS switch are edited here; one server `<P>` | Order: R20-0; R20-POP-BG, -FG, -OTHER, -NONE; R20-ARGS-ORDER; R20-CON-DESC, R20-CON-NOTEA (`allowed` leg and the `blocked` leg's pre-pattern read, no list change), R20-CON-CAP; then, each restored before the next: R20-ARGS-3 (JS off), R20-CON-4 and R20-CON-FLOOR (toolbar disarm), R20-CON-NOTEA's `blocked` leg (block list gains `s12blk.localhost`); R20-ARGS-OLD last (old build, then the current build back, `LOADED20 rerun=<k>`); R20-RESTORE; R20-LOGS. | | | |
| R20-0 ★ (R5.2; first row of slice A; the loaded-build proof of S1 and S2) | JS on, armed, as found | As §4.27 `R19-0`'s state reads and the reload (`recipes/reload-extension.ps1`, one re-run on exit 2, #99; exit 2 twice is the STOP `reload-failed`), then **before any state change**: (1) open `http://localhost:<P>/e2e-playground.html?s12=a1` with `newtab`; the SDK `tab.evaluate("({zeta: 1, alpha: 2, mid: {y: 1, x: 2}, list: [{q: 1, b: 2}]})")` and `tab.evaluate("(a) => a", {args: 7})`; (2) in the worker-console capture window (the wave's one DevTools exception) evaluate `chrome.runtime.getManifest().permissions.includes("webNavigation")` and `typeof chrome.webNavigation === "object" && chrome.webNavigation.onCreatedNavigationTarget.hasListeners()`; (3) `management.get` of the extension reads `enabled` and no `disabledReason`; (4) `git merge-base --is-ancestor b3cbe84 HEAD` (S4's code commit) | **The first line of the slice's record is §4.26's `MANIFEST …` line; the second is exactly:** `LOADED20 keys=page nested=page elem=page perm=yes listener=yes enabled=yes args=answered`; then `RULES 0`, `STATE armed=true evaluate=true …`, `TOGGLE reading on` or `off`, `FOUNDLIST blocks-fixtures=no`, `HEAD <12 hex> tree=clean`, `S4 commit=ancestor`. Page order is `zeta,alpha,mid,list`, nested `y,x`, element `q,b` (the value written above); Chrome's sorted order would be `alpha,list,mid,zeta`, `x,y`, `b,q` (`…-followups-steps/Stage0.md` § Measured facts, "Key order today") | `keys=page nested=page elem=page` (the value's key order through the SDK, S2: `extension/src/evaluate/serialize.ts`, `extension/test/s13-envelope.test.ts`), `perm=yes listener=yes` (S1: `extension/manifest.json`, `extension/test/manifest.test.ts`), `enabled=yes`, `args=answered` (the call returned `7`). **Why these discriminate:** a build before S2 returns `keys=sorted` (Chrome sorts the keys of a `returnByValue` object, Stage 0); a build before S1 has no `webNavigation` in the loaded manifest (`perm=no`, and `chrome.webNavigation` is undefined, `listener=no`); a stale worker that the reload did not replace shows both. The loaded manifest is read from the worker console, not from disk, because Chrome reads a manifest at load or reload and disk can differ from the loaded one; **`chrome://extensions` cannot show the permission** (it adds no warning line, #393), so the card is read only for `enabled`. **The MCP is not a proof of S2:** `browser_evaluate` prints the value's `description`, which already kept page order on the old build (`mcp/src/tools/registry.ts`; #391 (4)), so the MCP leg cannot tell builds apart and is not read here. If the console cannot evaluate, `perm` and `listener` print `unread` (`FALLBACK`: the disk manifest's `permissions` is read and recorded as such, which cannot show what is loaded); R20-0 is then PASS on the keys alone, the permission is NOT MEASURED, and R20-POP-BG becomes the discriminating proof of S1. `perm=no`, `keys=sorted` or `enabled=no`: **STOP `build-not-loaded` before any state change** (the reload did not take, root `CLAUDE.md`); `tree=clean` else STOP. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-0`. Source: phase doc 0.8 seed R20-0 and #391 (4), (5); Stage 0.md "Reload with `webNavigation` added"; `S1`/`S2` as-built contracts; decision #393 |

Three rounds: **before every round** B's `focusTab` on H (closing a popup tends to bring its opener O to the front), then the guard read `active-guard=bg` after it, so a round whose guard reads `fg` is NOT DISCRIMINATING and is not counted; A runs `O.evaluate("window.open('http://localhost:<P>/e2e-playground.html?popup=1') !== null")` (the `evaluate` carries the user activation); A and B both listen for `leaseGranted`; the popup is closed and released between rounds | `POP R20-POP-BG leg=bg n=3 granted-to=A in-ms=<m ≤ 1000> others=0 held-call=TAB_HELD active-guard=bg console-new=0`; B's call (`evaluate "1"`) on A's popup handle answers `TAB_HELD`, equal in code and message to B's control call on A's O handle (the daemon's own text; the control makes the comparison independent of its wording); A's call on its popup is `ok`; `ANSWER` for each | **3 of 3 rounds:** A receives exactly one `leaseGranted {reason: "opener"}` for the popup within **1 000 ms** (the join holds a `tabCreated` at most `OPENER_JOIN_HOLD_MS` = 250 ms, `extension/src/opener-join.ts`; Stage 0 measured the join in under 1 ms), B and every other client receive none (`others=0`), B's call on the popup is `TAB_HELD`, and the worker console printed no line meanwhile (`console-new=0`: the join logs nothing, no URL). **Discrimination:** before S1, `tabCreated.openerTabId` was Chrome's value, the window's *active* tab for a background opener (25 of 30, Stage 0), so the lease went to B, the holder of H (`granted-to=B`: the STOP `popup-lease-wrong` if it happens on this build; a granted-to-none outcome is a FAIL). The `active-guard=bg` read is what makes the row able to fail. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-pop-bg`. The allowed leg's `marker=0` is the *expected* reading, so `marker=1` would be a FAIL against the written limit (a contents read nobody built). | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-con-notea`. Source: decisions #387, #391 (3); Stage0.md "Replayed console arg"; S7-run-record §4 item 2, §6 |
| R20-CON-CAP ★ (S3b, #398; the cap keeps the **newest** lines; re-states R19-CON-CAP's direction) | JS on, armed | On `…/s12-console.html?s12=cap` (200 messages of 2 000 characters): the MCP `browser_console_messages {}`; the SDK `tab.consoleMessages()` for the entry count; `tab.consoleMessages({limit: 3})` as the shortest-cap control | `CONCAP lines=<k+1> body-chars=<c> marker=yes first-index=<200-k+1> last-index=200 sentinel=<s>`; the **first line** of the MCP text is `… [truncated: <200-k> older lines cut — narrow with level/limit]` (`sdk/src/format/console-messages.ts`; `sdk/test/s12-console-cap.test.ts`: `OLDER(n)`), the kept lines follow oldest first | The marker is the first line, exactly once, with `n = 200 - k` where `k` = the kept lines; `body-chars` (the kept lines joined by newlines, without the marker) **≤ 20 000**; the kept lines are the **newest contiguous block** (`last-index=200`, indices consecutive); the SDK's count is 200; no line is cut mid-line. Arithmetic, before the run: a kept line is a 30-character time-and-level prefix plus 2 000 characters (2 030), so `k = 9` (nine lines are 18 278 characters, ten are 20 309) and the marker reads `191 older lines cut`; a different `k` with the same rule held is RECORD with the line length. `limit: 3` returns the last 3 entries and no marker. **Discrimination:** an uncapped formatter answers 400 000 characters; the Run 19 formatter kept the *oldest* nine (`first-index=1`, marker `… more lines, the newest …`), which fails `last-index=200` and the marker text. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-con-cap` (a Run 20 counterpart of `concapStats`, which pins the old marker). Source: decision #398; S3b as-built; `sdk/src/format/console-messages.ts` |
| R20-CON-4 ★ (re-measures **R19-CON-4**'s MCP leg, NOT DISCRIMINATING on a reader of the extension's text) | **disarmed** (one toolbar press, off, then on again by the row) | As R19-CON-4: on the `s12=con` page, the toolbar button pressed (off, title `safety: disarmed by the user`); `tab.consoleMessages()`; the MCP `browser_console_messages {}`; control `tab.snapshot()` and `browser_snapshot`; the button pressed (on); one read after arming | **SDK leg:** `CONREF R20-CON-4 disarmed code=DISARMED exact=yes data=keys:scope same-as=snapshot`, `data {"scope":"all"}`, message exactly `The user has turned the bridge off in Chrome, so every page call is refused. Stop and ask the user to turn it back on; do not retry.` (`extension/src/safety.ts`, `DISARMED_ALL_MESSAGE`). **MCP leg:** `MCPLINE R20-CON-4 leg=mcp is-error=yes text=exact lines=1` with the text exactly `DISARMED: the user has turned the bridge off in Chrome. Stop and ask the user to turn it back on; do not retry.` (`sdk/src/error.ts`, `DISARMED_ALL_LINE`), equal to the `browser_snapshot` control's text | SDK: exact code, message and `data`, equal to the snapshot control's; 0 entries. MCP: the line and `isError` equal to the control's. The read after arming answers `ok` (the tool is alive). **Discrimination:** a build that reads while disarmed answers `ok` with entries; a reader that expects the extension's message on the MCP leg gets `text=other` and the row is NOT DISCRIMINATING, not a pass. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-con-4`. Source: Run 19 record §4 item 8, §6; `mcp/test/safety.test.ts` |
| R20-CON-FLOOR ★ (re-measures **R19-CON-FLOOR**, NOT DISCRIMINATING: the driver kept 3 s and 2.5 s waits; the amended rule is #361's) | **disarmed** then re-armed | As R19-CON-FLOOR on `…/s12-console.html?s12=tick`: ticks run 2 s armed; toolbar press (off), **wait 5 s** (the page keeps ticking and throwing; no bridge call); toolbar press (on); **wait 3.5 s**; one `tab.consoleMessages()`; then the page's `window.__s12.ticks` and `.excs` lengths and their times | `CONFLOOR disarmed-ticks=<n≥3> in-reply-disarmed=0 after-ticks=<n≥2> in-reply-after=<same n> exc-disarmed=<n≥3> exc-in-reply-disarmed=0 exc-after=<m≥2> exc-in-reply-after=<same m>` | As R19-CON-FLOOR's pass rule word for word (`in-reply-disarmed = 0`, `in-reply-after = after-ticks`, the same for the uncaught errors; ticks within 500 ms of a press are not judged) **with the preconditions now reachable**: `disarmed-ticks ≥ 3` and `after-ticks ≥ 2` are checked first and, if the waits did not produce them, the row is NOT MEASURED "precondition", never a pass. The helper prints the two waits it used (`wait-off-ms`, `wait-on-ms`); a wait below 5 000 or 3 500 is a helper error (STOP before the row). **Discrimination:** a build with no floor returns the disarmed ticks; one that floors console calls but not exceptions returns the disarmed errors. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-con-floor`. SDK and raw legs: `OLD R20-ARGS-OLD leg=<sdk or ref or file or raw> code=VERSION hint=yes forwarded=<no on raw, n/a elsewhere>` with the message `the Chrome extension is an older build without evaluate args — reload it at chrome://extensions (↻ on Browser Agent Bridge)` (`daemon/src/intercept.ts`, `ARGS_RELOAD_HINT`). **MCP leg:** `MCPLINE R20-ARGS-OLD leg=mcp is-error=yes text=exact lines=1` with that same text as the bare message | Every `args` call refused with the hint; the raw leg `forwarded=no`; the MCP leg read by its own line (`text=exact`, `isError`), never a code on line 1; the no-`args` call `ok`. **After the row the current build is rebuilt, reloaded and `LOADED20 rerun=<k>` printed with the full R20-0 values, else STOP `old-build-left-loaded`.** The raw leg's "nothing forwarded" clause is not told apart live from a daemon that forwards and rewrites the answer (Run 19 note #365): it is proved by `mcp/test/s12-args-cross.test.ts` and printed here as `forwarded=no` for the record. **If the old build cannot be loaded within one re-run (exit 2 twice), the row is `NOT RUN — old build not loadable`; it never counts as a pass.** | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-args-old` (the scratch-worktree build and swap of Run 19's `s12-oldbuild` helper, read-only). The MCP leg is read by its own text and `isError`. **Discrimination:** before S4 the second call answered the overlap text of `describeDebuggerError` (`Chrome DevTools (or another extension) is already attached to this tab — Chrome allows one debugger per tab. | R20-CHOOSE-REFUSE-DIS ★ (re-measures **R19-CHOOSE-REFUSE** leg `dis`, NOT DISCRIMINATING: the page's handler clicked a disabled input, which opens nothing, #370) | ROOT added | **Reachability, stated first (#370, #391 (7)):** the refusal `the file input is disabled` (`extension/src/content/upload-target.ts`, `DISABLED_INPUT_MESSAGE`; the chooser path reads `attrs.has("disabled")` in `extension/src/upload-files.ts`) is reachable on the chooser path only if Chrome emits `Page.fileChooserOpened` for an input that is **enabled at the click and disabled when the extension reads it**. Whether Chrome does is not known before the run. **Leg `flip` (the primary):** on `s13-chooser.html?s13=disflip` the chooser call (`<ROOT>\s11-1k.bin`, by the button's locator). **Leg `self` (the second try):** on `s13=disself`, the call targets the disabled input itself (`css:#dis`). **Leg `btn` (the control of the statement):** on `s13=disbtn` a button that clicks an input disabled from the start (Run 19's `dis` shape) | Leg `flip`, **if the refusal is reachable:** `CHOOSE R20-CHOOSE-REFUSE-DIS flip BAD_PARAMS exact=yes files=0 change=0 clicks=1 …` with exactly `the file input is disabled`, no `data`, no path. The legs run in the order `btn`, `flip`, `self`, each on a fresh load, and the row's verdict is decided by `flip` alone unless it answers `TIMEOUT`: then `self` decides, `BAD_PARAMS` with exactly the message = PASS (the refusal is reachable by the input itself, `flip` recorded), `TIMEOUT` = RECORD, `ok` with `files=1` = FAIL. **Any answer of `flip` other than `BAD_PARAMS` with that message, `TIMEOUT` or `ok files=1`** (another code, another message, a message with `data` or a path) is a FAIL with the answer recorded verbatim. **Discrimination:** the refusal text can only come from the extension's post-click read; `btn` rules out a refusal that is a leftover of the page. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-choose-refuse-dis`; new fixture page.
