(1) `evaluate "1 + 1"` answers `2` (an earlier attach); (2) wait until the bridge's kept session is gone: `IDLE ≥ 20 000 ms` since that reply; (3) **control, before-open:** `CTRL20 … when=before-open windows=0 title=none attached=no`; (4) DevTools opened into X and **undocked** (the helper's method: the Run 19 app-menu opener of `recipes/CLAUDE.md`, then the dock-side change; a method that cannot undock leaves `windows=0` and the leg unreached); (5) **control, after-open, read before any bridge call:** `CTRL20 … when=after-open windows=1 title=ok attached=yes`; (6) the bridge's call `tab.evaluate("1 + 1")`; (7) DevTools closed by the wave, `devtools-windows-after` read | `CTRL20 R20-DEVT-UNDOCK leg=undock when=before-open windows=0 title=none attached=no targets=<n>`; `… when=after-open windows=1 title=ok attached=yes targets=<n>`; `DEVT20 R20-DEVT-UNDOCK leg=undock answer=ok value=ok exact=n/a devtools-windows-after=0` (the value `2`). Stage 0 measured `ok` 4 of 4 for this condition (`stage0-m2-D.txt` D1, scratch profile) | **PASS (matches Stage 0):** control passes **and** the call answers `ok` with `2`: undocked DevTools does not refuse the bridge's attach. **If the call answers `DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`** with exactly `Chrome DevTools (or another extension) is already attached to this tab — Chrome allows one debugger per tab. Close DevTools on that tab and retry.` (`extension/src/debugger-errors.ts`): **FAIL against the expected `ok`, recorded as the finding `devtools-refuses` with the leg named**, no STOP; the conditional rows below then run under this leg. **If the control cannot be read or the window is not undocked: RECORD `devtools-control-unreadable`**; if `attached=no` after the open: NOT MEASURED "DevTools was not attached". Never a PASS without the control. **Discrimination:** Run 19's DEVPROBE took `attached` from the refusal code, so an `ok` could not be told from "DevTools never attached"; the window title and `getTargets()` read before the call are independent of the bridge. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-devt-undock`. Source: #391 (1); Stage0.md D1, `stage0-m2-D.txt`; decision #369; phase doc 0.8 |
| R20-DEVT-EARLY ★ (re-measures **#369 condition 2**; DevTools opened before the bridge's first attach) | JS on, armed; window X | A fresh tab in X that the bridge has not attached to (the tab is created by A's `newTab` or loaded by hand; no CDP call on it). **Legs `undocked` and `docked`**, each on its own fresh tab: (0) **control, before-open** (the proof the bridge has not attached): `CTRL20 … when=before-open … attached=no` (a docked leg `windows=n/a title=n/a`); (1) DevTools opened into X (undocked for the first leg, docked for the second); (2) the control, before any bridge call: `CTRL20 … when=after-open …` as above (a docked leg: `windows=n/a title=n/a`, judged by `attached=yes` alone); (3) the **first** bridge CDP call on the tab, `tab.evaluate("1 + 1")`; (4) DevTools closed by the wave | `DEVT20 R20-DEVT-EARLY leg=undocked answer=ok value=ok …`; `DEVT20 R20-DEVT-EARLY leg=docked answer=ok value=ok …` (Stage 0: undocked `ok` 3 of 3 valid, docked `ok` 3 of 3, D2) | As R20-DEVT-UNDOCK, per leg: PASS = both controls (`before-open attached=no`, `after-open attached=yes`) + `ok` + `2`; a `before-open` read of `attached=yes` means the bridge or another debugger was already attached: NOT MEASURED "the tab was attached before the open"; the refusal text is a FAIL-with-finding for that leg and its conditional rows run under that leg; an unreadable control is RECORD; the row's verdict is the worse of its two legs, each printed. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-devt-early`. Source: #391 (1); Stage0.md D2; decision #369 |
| R20-UP-DEVT, R20-KEEP-4, R20-SWEEP-HELD ★ (conditional re-runs of **R19-UP-DEVT**, **R19-KEEP-4**, **R19-SWEEP-HELD** under the refusing condition) | **Run only if** R20-DEVT-UNDOCK or R20-DEVT-EARLY printed the finding `devtools-refuses` for a leg; ROOT added for UP-DEVT only; window X | Under the **refusing leg's** DevTools state, opened by the same method with the control read before each call. **UP-DEVT:** as §4.26 `R18-UP-DEVT`: control `evaluate` answers K-devt; `tab.upload({css:"#up-single"}, <ROOT>\s11-1k.bin)` on `s11-upload.html` answers K-devt; DevTools closed; control `evaluate` `ok`. **KEEP-4:** on `s12=con` one `consoleMessages` (a session is kept); DevTools opened before the keeper's 5 s idle bound; `tab.consoleMessages()` again; DevTools closed; again. **SWEEP-HELD (last):** DevTools open, the extension reloaded by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1`, the tab re-claimed, `evaluate "1"`; DevTools closed; `evaluate "1"`; R20-0C re-run as `LOADED20 rerun=<k>` | As the R19/R18 rows: UP-DEVT K-devt, K-devt, `ok`; KEEP-4 `KEEPDT … leg=devtools next=DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS`, `leg=closed next=ok` with 11 entries; SWEEP-HELD `SWEEPHELD after-reload=DEBUGGER_DEVTOOLS devtools-open=yes reclaimed=yes`, then `ok`. K-devt is the text quoted in R20-DEVT-UNDOCK | The pass rules of R18-UP-DEVT, R19-KEEP-4 and R19-SWEEP-HELD word for word, **with the DevTools control of this sheet read before each call** (a K-devt answer no longer stands as the proof that DevTools is attached). A window X still open at the slice's end is the STOP `devtools-left-open`. **When no condition refused, each of the three prints `ROW <id> NOT RUN — no DevTools condition refused` and the record carries the finding "both conditions answer `ok`; #374 item 3's reword waits for the user" (recorded, not a failure, phase doc "Continues anyway").** The `webNavigation` reload in SWEEP-HELD is the reason it runs last. | S5-helpers: `R20 r20-up-devt`, `r20-keep-4`, `r20-sweep-held`. **Slices and order (#271, #384).** Three live slices, each **self-contained**: its own proof row (R20-0, R20-0B, R20-0C), its own R20-RESTORE and R20-LOGS, its own console capture started before its first row, its own `PORT` line. The prologue of each is §4.27's (preflight, server, the files `<ROOT>`, `<OUTSIDE>`, `s11-1k.bin`, `s11-sent.bin`, the sentinel folder, `npm run build` from a clean tree at a committed HEAD, the reload by `recipes/reload-extension.ps1`, one re-run on exit 2, #99). **R20-0 is the first row of a slice and proves the loaded build before any state change; R20-RESTORE then R20-LOGS end every slice**; a row that changes the safety state restores it before the next row. Runner attempts: at most three per slice (phase doc § S5; the fix rounds of agent-rules §7 apply). One live slice at a time (#384).
- **liveA20 (R20-0, POP, ARGS, CON; JS on, armed):** R20-0; R20-POP-BG, R20-POP-FG, R20-POP-OTHER, R20-POP-NONE; R20-ARGS-ORDER; R20-CON-DESC, R20-CON-NOTEA (`allowed` leg and the `blocked` leg's pre-pattern read), R20-CON-CAP; then, each restored before the next: R20-ARGS-3 (JS off), R20-CON-4 and R20-CON-FLOOR (toolbar disarm), R20-CON-NOTEA's `blocked` leg (block list); **R20-ARGS-OLD last** (the old build, then the current build and `LOADED20 rerun=<k>`); R20-RESTORE; R20-LOGS. The only DevTools window is the worker-console capture. 15 ids.
- **liveB20 (CHOOSE, G2; JS on, armed; ROOT added):** R20-0B; the row-less `CLOSERPROBE`; R20-CHOOSE-TWICE, R20-CHOOSE-KEPT, R20-CHOOSE-REFUSE-DIS; R20-G2-1; **R20-VERSION last**; R20-RESTORE; R20-LOGS. 8 ids and one probe.
- **liveC20 (the DevTools rows alone; JS on, armed; window X):** R20-0C; `FLAG`; R20-DEVT-UNDOCK, R20-DEVT-EARLY; the three conditional rows; R20-RESTORE; R20-LOGS. 8 ids and the `FLAG` record line; no other row opens a DevTools window.
- **Rows that may end RECORD, NOT MEASURED or NOT RUN, stated up front:** `R20-0`'s `perm`/`listener` (console cannot evaluate: `unread`, NOT MEASURED, POP-BG proves S1); R20-POP-OTHER `unjoined` (NOT MEASURED, no such tab can be made) and `human` (UIA click unavailable); R20-POP-NONE `blocked` (RECORD if Chrome allowed the popup); R20-CON-NOTEA's cut-path clause (NOT REACHABLE, S3a not run); R20-CHOOSE-TWICE's MCP leg (NOT MEASURED if the MCP serialises); R20-CHOOSE-KEPT leg `same` (a record by design); R20-CHOOSE-REFUSE-DIS (RECORD if Chrome emits no chooser for a flipped or self-targeted input); R20-G2-1 legs (NOT DISCRIMINATING if B alone cannot be captured); R20-ARGS-OLD and R20-VERSION (NOT RUN if the old build cannot be loaded); R20-DEVT-UNDOCK and -EARLY (RECORD if the control cannot be read or the window cannot be undocked; NOT MEASURED if the control reads `attached=no`; the three conditional rows NOT RUN if neither condition refused).
- **STOP lines matched to a return (R5.6):** every `STOP <reason>` line is followed by one `RETURN <seq> after-stop=<reason> restored=<yes or no or n/a> readback=<equal or n/a>` before the next row; the reasons and where they arise: `build-not-loaded` and `extension-disabled` and `reload-failed` (R20-0, -0B, -0C); `popup-lease-wrong` (R20-POP-BG, -FG, -OTHER, -NONE); `existing-tool-changed` (R20-ARGS-ORDER's controls, R20-CHOOSE-TWICE's and R20-CHOOSE-KEPT's control upload, R20-VERSION's control); `args-ran-js-off` (R20-ARGS-3); `chooser-interception-left-on` and `outside-file-reached-page` (R20-CHOOSE-*, R20-VERSION); `dialog-left-open` (the row-less `CLOSERPROBE`, R20-CHOOSE-*, R20-RESTORE) and `devtools-left-open` (R20-DEVT-*, the conditional rows, R20-RESTORE); `not-restored`; `text-leak` (R20-LOGS); `old-build-left-loaded` (R20-ARGS-OLD, R20-VERSION, R20-RESTORE); `throw-after-state` (any slice). R5.6 passes when every `STOP` has exactly one `RETURN` after it, in order, and each slice's final ledger holds no unmatched line either way.

*Dated note (2026-10-30, before any liveB20 observation; Step 13 S5-addendum407, decision #407):* R20-POP-BG-3, R20-POP-FG-3 and R20-POP-OTHER-3 below are written per #407 before any liveB20 observation; no Chrome, daemon or fixture was run to write them. liveA20's R20-POP-BG, -FG and -OTHER FAILs and #406 stay as recorded; the `-2` ids of #404 are withdrawn and never used; G1.4 is judged on R20-POP-BG-3. Each -3 row copies its original cell for cell and changes only its id, its line ids and cross references, the three clauses below, leg `third`'s W2 note and its helper name; every other clause, the lease clause, `others=0`, the active guard, the 1 000 ms bound, the 3-of-3 rule, the discrimination text, the `unjoined` leg and the STOP `popup-lease-wrong` stand as in the originals. **Order:** liveB20 runs the three rows after R20-0B and before the row-less `CLOSERPROBE`, in the order R20-POP-BG-3, R20-POP-FG-3, R20-POP-OTHER-3; liveB20 becomes 11 ids and one probe; the STOP `popup-lease-wrong` arises in the three -3 rows too; R20-POP-OTHER-3's `unjoined` leg and its `human` leg are added to the up-front NOT MEASURED list above. **Line forms** (they replace form (3)'s `console-new` for these rows only): `POP <row> leg=<label> n=<popups> granted-to=<A or B or C or none> in-ms=<max over n or n/a> others=<n> held-call=<TAB_HELD or ok or n/a> holder=<A or other or n/a> active-guard=<bg or fg> console-join=<n or unread> console-url=<n or unread>` (expected on a passing leg: `held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A console-join=0 console-url=0`); `CHECK <row> <leg> console-new=<n> RECORD`; for leg `human` one `CHECK R20-POP-OTHER-3 human round=<k> popup-opened=<yes or no> opener=<O or other or none> granted-to=<A or B or C or none>` per click. FG's `R15-POP-2 (a)` record line and every bound (1 000 ms, 3 of 3, 250 ms) are unchanged. **Clause (1), held-call:** B's call on the popup answers code `TAB_HELD` and its message names A's client as the holder (the message contains `held by "<A's client name>"`, the name A gave in its hello, as §4.6 POP-JOIN-2 asks); B's control call on O answers code `TAB_HELD` too; the handle and the time in the message are not compared; the record prints the holder as a label (`holder=A`), never the message. **Clause (2), console, judged by content and not by count:** no worker-console line printed during the leg comes from the opener join, and none holds the popup's URL or any part of it; the count is a RECORD line only. The capture is the slice's worker-console capture as the wave's helper saves it (`Save-S11bConsole`): one capture line per UI Automation element name in the DevTools window, so a log line may appear whole or as pieces. Content is judged over the whole capture as saved after the leg (the console's rendered rows, which may not reach back to the slice's start), not over a before/after difference, so there is no prefix rule and no `unread` from a shifted list. A capture line "comes from the opener join" when it names the join (`OpenerJoin`, `opener-join`, `opener_join` or `OPENER_JOIN`, case-insensitive; the extension bundle is not minified, so an error thrown in the join names its class). The content check is a backstop to the static fact that `extension/src/opener-join.ts` has no log call (the log-site list is `extension/test/log-allowlist.ts`, which `extension/test/log-scan.test.ts` enforces), so no product log line can come from the join. "Any part of the popup's URL" is any of `localhost:<P>` (the slice's real port), `e2e-playground.html?popup`, `popup=1`, case-insensitive, on any capture line. **Liveness control:** the clause can pass only on a capture that shows the worker's lines (a count above 0 fails it on any capture): the worker prints `[bridge] evaluate <n> chars` only for calls that reach the extension: A's `window.open` evaluate on O (one per round in legs `bg`, `fg`, `third`), B's own `evaluate "1"` on its tab in leg `third`, and A's call on its popup; B's calls on the popup and on O are refused `TAB_HELD` by the daemon and print nothing. So A's call on its popup (the original's "A's call on its popup is `ok`") is an `evaluate` whose expression length is unique within the slice (for example `1+0`, `1+0+0`, and so on, one `+0` more per call), made in every round whose popup opened, after B's held-call probe, so identical consecutive lines cannot be grouped into one. The control holds when the after-leg capture holds more `[bridge] evaluate` pieces than the capture saved just before the leg; `console-join` and `console-url` always print their counts, and a count above 0 is a FAIL whatever the control reads; if the control does not hold, `unread` replaces only a count of 0, and then turns a leg that would otherwise PASS into NOT MEASURED (a stated instrument limit). Every other clause is judged first: a STOP or a FAIL stands whatever the console reads; a round with `popup-opened=yes` and `granted-to=none` is a FAIL whatever the console reads. `console-new` (the RECORD count) is the capture's line count after the leg minus before it, never judged. Console text is never printed, only counts; a piece such as `[bridge] evaluate` passes the clause. **Clause (3), leg `human` only:** after each click the record states whether a popup tab was created (`popup-opened=yes|no`); `granted-to=none` is a FAIL only when a popup opened and NOT MEASURED when none did. `popup-opened=yes` whenever a `listTabs` row appears after the click that was absent from the row set read just before the click and that no client opened (no `newTab` in flight), read at or after the click's `sent` stamp + 1 000 ms, so the read cannot precede the grant; its opener is recorded, not judged (`opener=<O or other or none>`: Chrome's `openerTabId` on the row, compared with O's tab id). A round with `popup-opened=no` is NOT MEASURED and is not counted. The leg is FAIL if any round has a popup opened and `granted-to=none` (a product finding, #407's Revisit-if: back to the user) or a `held-call` not as clause (1); it is the STOP `popup-lease-wrong` if a popup is granted to B or C; it is PASS only when all three rounds opened a popup and each meets the clauses, else NOT MEASURED. B's held-call probe on the popup runs only in a round whose popup opened (`held-call=n/a` otherwise). If the UIA click cannot be sent the leg stays NOT MEASURED as in the original. **Leg `third`, W2 (#407):** B is put in its own window W2 if it can be made, as the sheet asks; if it cannot, the record prints `CHECK R20-POP-OTHER-3 third b-window=W (sheet: W2)` and the leg is judged by its clauses unchanged.*

| Row | Re-measures / state | Steps and control | Expected text (source) | Pass rule, discrimination, control, restore | Helper / source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R20-POP-BG-3 ★ (G1.4; #385; a background opener's popup is leased to its holder; new feature) — re-measures R20-POP-BG per #407 | JS on, armed; clients A (SDK), B (SDK) | One window W holds two fixture tabs: **O** = `…/e2e-playground.html?s12=a1` claimed by A, **H** = `…/e2e-playground.html?s12=bc` claimed by B, and **H is the window's active tab** (B's `focusTab` on H; the helper reads `listTabs` and proves `active-guard=bg` for O: if O is the active tab the row is NOT DISCRIMINATING, not a pass). Three rounds: **before every round** B's `focusTab` on H (closing a popup tends to bring its opener O to the front), then the guard read `active-guard=bg` after it, so a round whose guard reads `fg` is NOT DISCRIMINATING and is not counted; A runs `O.evaluate("window.open('http://localhost:<P>/e2e-playground.html?popup=1') !== null")` (the `evaluate` carries the user activation); A and B both listen for `leaseGranted`; the popup is closed and released between rounds | `POP R20-POP-BG-3 leg=bg n=3 granted-to=A in-ms=<m ≤ 1000> others=0 held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A active-guard=bg console-join=0 console-url=0`; `CHECK R20-POP-BG-3 bg console-new=<n> RECORD`; B's call (`evaluate "1"`) on A's popup handle answers code `TAB_HELD` and its message names A's client as the holder (the message contains `held by "<A's client name>"`, the name A gave in its hello), and B's control call on A's O handle answers code `TAB_HELD` too (the handle and the time in the message are not compared; the record prints `holder=A`, never the message); A's call on its popup is `ok`; `ANSWER` for each | **3 of 3 rounds:** A receives exactly one `leaseGranted {reason: "opener"}` for the popup within **1 000 ms** (the join holds a `tabCreated` at most `OPENER_JOIN_HOLD_MS` = 250 ms, `extension/src/opener-join.ts`; Stage 0 measured the join in under 1 ms), B and every other client receive none (`others=0`), B's call on the popup answers `TAB_HELD` and its message names A's client as the holder (`held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A`; B's control call on O answers `TAB_HELD` too), and no worker-console line printed during the leg comes from the opener join and none holds the popup's URL or any part of it (`console-join=0 console-url=0`; the count `console-new` is a RECORD line only, the join logs nothing). **Discrimination:** before S1, `tabCreated.openerTabId` was Chrome's value, the window's *active* tab for a background opener (25 of 30, Stage 0), so the lease went to B, the holder of H (`granted-to=B`: the STOP `popup-lease-wrong` if it happens on this build; a granted-to-none outcome is a FAIL). The `active-guard=bg` read is what makes the row able to fail. | S5-drivers-B4: `R20 r20-pop-bg-3`. Source: phase doc 0.8 and G1.4; spec §10 "Note 2026-10-29 (Step 13 S1)"; decision #385, #394; §4.6 POP-JOIN-2; decision #407 |
| R20-POP-FG-3 ★ (the foreground case, as today; timed against R15-POP-2 (a)) — re-measures R20-POP-FG per #407 | JS on, armed; clients A, B | As R20-POP-BG-3, with **O brought to the front of W first** (A's `focusTab` on O; `active-guard=fg`): A holds O, B holds H behind it. Three rounds as above | `POP R20-POP-FG-3 leg=fg n=3 granted-to=A in-ms=<m ≤ 1000> others=0 held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A active-guard=fg console-join=0 console-url=0`; `CHECK R20-POP-FG-3 fg console-new=<n> RECORD`; the record also prints `R15-POP-2 (a) leaseGranted 150 ms` (§5, Run 15) beside `in-ms` as a recorded comparison | Same clauses as R20-POP-BG-3, 3 of 3. The **comparison with R15-POP-2 (a)** is a record line, not judged: before S1 the foreground grant took 150 ms (Run 15); the join may add its wait, so a larger `in-ms` is expected to stay under 400 ms (150 + the 250 ms bound) and is judged only by the 1 000 ms bound. **Discrimination:** this leg also passes before S1 (the active tab was O); it is the *regression control* that the join did not break the foreground path, and its pass proves nothing about S1 by itself (a build that never grants fails it). | S5-drivers-B4: `R20 r20-pop-fg-3`. Source: phase doc 0.8, #391 (2); §5 Run 15 (§4.22 `R15-POP-2` (a)); decision #407 |
| R20-POP-OTHER-3 ★ (#391 (2); a popup whose source tab another client holds; a third client holds the window's active tab) — re-measures R20-POP-OTHER per #407 | JS on, armed; clients A, B, C; two windows W (A's O and C's H) and W2 (B's tab) | **Leg `third`:** A holds O in W (behind), C holds H = W's active tab, B holds a tab in W2; B makes its own bridge calls (an `evaluate "1"` every 300 ms on its tab; B is put in its own window W2 if it can be made, as the sheet asks, and if it cannot the record prints `CHECK R20-POP-OTHER-3 third b-window=W (sheet: W2)` and the leg is judged by its clauses unchanged) while A opens the popup from O as in R20-POP-BG (**before every round** C's `focusTab` on H, then the `active-guard=bg` read after it; a round that reads `fg` is NOT DISCRIMINATING and not counted). **Leg `human` (no bridge caller at all):** the wave's own UIA click on the `#open-popup` link of O (O in front of W, held by A; the helper's human-style click of §4.27 `R19-CHOOSE-4`, plain, no modifier), B and C idle. Three rounds each; popups closed between rounds | `POP R20-POP-OTHER-3 leg=third n=3 granted-to=A in-ms=<m ≤ 1000> others=0 held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A active-guard=bg console-join=0 console-url=0`; `POP R20-POP-OTHER-3 leg=human n=3 granted-to=A … held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A active-guard=fg console-join=0 console-url=0`, and for leg `human` one `CHECK R20-POP-OTHER-3 human round=<k> popup-opened=<yes or no> opener=<O or other or none> granted-to=<A or B or C or none>` per click; each leg also prints `CHECK R20-POP-OTHER-3 <leg> console-new=<n> RECORD` | **Each leg 3 of 3:** the lease goes to A only, whoever is the caller or the Chrome-named opener (the daemon grants to the holder of the tab named by `openerTabId`, never to the caller: spec §10 S1 note; `daemon/src/intercept.ts`, unchanged). B's own traffic during the popup does not move the grant. Each leg carries R20-POP-BG-3's clauses (`held-call=TAB_HELD holder=A`, `console-join=0 console-url=0`), B's held-call probe on the popup running only in a round whose popup opened (`held-call=n/a` otherwise). **Leg `human` (#407):** a round whose click created no popup tab (`popup-opened=no`) is NOT MEASURED and not counted; `granted-to=none` with a popup opened is a FAIL (a product finding); the leg is PASS only when all three rounds opened a popup and each meets the clauses, else NOT MEASURED. **Discrimination:** `third` is the leg a fallback to Chrome's opener fails (the grant goes to C, the active tab's holder: `popup-lease-wrong`); `human` shows the grant needs no caller (a grant tied to the calling client would give none). **Pre-declared unreachable: the `unjoined` leg** (a tab Chrome gave an `openerTabId` and no navigation target, #394) cannot be made on a fixture page: Stage 0 found `tabs.create({openerTabId})` gives no `openerTabId`, Ctrl+click and middle-click tabs join 10 of 10, and a blocked popup sends neither event; the leg is printed `ROW R20-POP-OTHER-3 unjoined NOT MEASURED — no unjoined tab can be made` and does not change the row's verdict. If the helper cannot send the human-style click (UIA fails), leg `human` is NOT MEASURED. | S5-drivers-B4: `R20 r20-pop-other-3`. Source: phase doc 0.8, #391 (2); Stage0.md "Ctrl+click, middle click"; #394; decision #407 |

*Dated note (2026-10-30, before any liveB20 or liveC20 observation; Step 13 S5-drivers-C4, decision #416):* **R20-RESTORE's slice-C dock clause.** When R20-0C's found side is `unread` and no row of the slice changed the dock side (the driver tracks this explicitly with one flag set before every call that can change the side, never by inference from NOT RUN lines), the clause prints `DOCK R20-RESTORE side=<read-back>`, `CHECK R20-RESTORE found-side=unread read-back=<read-back> NOT MEASURED found side unread, nothing changed` and the note `ROW R20-RESTORE dock NOT MEASURED found side unread, nothing changed` (a leg note like R20-POP-OTHER's `unjoined` line, not counted as a row by the aggregator; the row's own line then reads `ROW R20-RESTORE NOT MEASURED slice=C`), so the row's verdict is NOT MEASURED "found side unread, nothing changed" whatever the read-back, and the slice goes on to R20-LOGS (exit 0, no STOP). If any dock change ran in the slice (a leg's open with a side, KEEP-4's pre-open, the restore's own set), an unread or different side stays the STOP `not-restored` as the row above says. A found side that was read is judged as before. The driver passes `--changed yes|no`; anything other than exactly `no` counts as changed. No expected text or pass rule other than the one named here changes, and no observation was made to write this note.

*Dated note (2026-10-30, before any liveB20 or liveC20 observation; Step 13 S5-drivers-C4, decision #416):* **The capture window.** R20-RESTORE's DevTools count (`devtools=` of the `DIALOGS` line) leaves out the wave's worker-console capture window; R20-LOGS then saves the capture; the slice's teardown or a STOP closes it, as slices A and B already do. No expected text or pass rule other than the one named here changes, and no observation was made to write this note.

*Dated note (2026-10-30, before any liveB20 or liveC20 observation; Step 13 S5-drivers-C4, decision #416):* **The DIALOGS label.** Slice B prints `DIALOGS B native=… devtools=… wave-windows-open=…`, as the slice B row above writes it, with the same counts and the same two STOPs as before; slice A's recorded line `DIALOGS liveA20 …` is read as `DIALOGS A` in the record (a label, not a judged value). No expected text or pass rule other than the one named here changes, and no observation was made to write this note.

### Past runs

The records of Runs 1–20 (2026-06 to 2026-10) are in tag `archive/dev-history`:
`git show archive/dev-history:docs/e2e-test-plan.md`, § 5. A summary per step is in `docs/history.md`.

## 6. Exit criteria

- **All ★ must-pass cases pass** → the bridge is proven end-to-end; merge `feat/agent-bridge` → `main`.
- Any ★ failure → capture the diagnostics (§2) into the notes and we fix before merging.
- Non-★ failures are logged as roadmap items (see `docs/progress-and-roadmap.md`) unless they block a ★ case.
